The tests compare the finder with the independent enumeration of the *same prefix* (`referenceFinned` with `limit`: the first removing pattern inside it, its position plus one as the work, `capHit: 'work'` exactly when a combination was left over), not with an unrestricted oracle whose later witness would turn an allowed truncation into a failure (the enumeration always gets an effective limit: the one given, else the registered finder's own 50,000; unrestricted enumerations appear only in separate, explicit checks), and keep the complete first-pattern agreement on uncapped scans. **Structural upper bounds** (units per call, digits × 2 orientations × triples of complete lines, every triple possible, with the largest number of cover trials inside one unit): 32 on 4×4 (4 trials), 240 on 6×6 (20), 1,512 on every 9×9 board (84), 5,280 on the 12-digit board (220), 17,920 on the 16-digit board (560), 128,520 on the Sohei and the Butterfly and 255,420 on the Samurai and the Flower (2,925 trials); on four boards the bound is above the limit, **and it is a bound, not an observation**: the candidate ownership leaves far fewer eligible lines, and exceeding 50,000 in this table does not make a cap hit. **Observed** (`tests/artifacts/difficulty-v3/MS09b/work-census-n3.js`, the registered finder with its own limit; the source, corpus and seed of every row are in `docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS09b/work-census.json`; finder measurements of this machine in one process, not a GP or qualification verdict): **3,508 sampled calls** (the states of the agreement setups: 1,200 classic9 states of seed 99, 400 of seed 777, 300 of seed 20260101, 400 classic6, 300 classic4, 60 twelve-digit, 32 sixteen-digit, 120 each of Diagonal, Hyper, Jigsaw 9, Killer, Greater-Than and Wordoku, and 12 each of the Samurai, the Sohei, the Butterfly and the Flower for the seeds 4242 and 777) **and none reached the limit**. The largest work per cohort is 567 on classic9 (seed 99: p95 0.697 ms, max 2.163 ms), 4,342 on the 16-digit board (p95 21.813 ms, max 23.382 ms over its 32 calls), 8,412 on the Sohei (seed 777: p95 and max 70.517 ms of its 12 calls), 23,466 on the Samurai (47 % of the limit; seed 4242: p95 and max 212.353 ms of its 12 calls) and 567 on the Flower; the five fixtures cost 86 to 338 units. **Eight constructed calls** (two floods on each of the four multi-grid boards) are kept apart from them: the only cap hit of the census is the blank Samurai with every digit open (52,650 combinations, 50,000 tested, 3.781 s, `null` with `capHit: 'work'`); the blank Sohei (14,688 units) costs 0.948 s, one open digit on the Samurai (5,850 units) 0.444 s. The limit was not adjusted and the finned X-Wing's was not touched; the multi-grid latency is the named item S-AV31 (check-list section 2), routed to MS18d with an MS18e audit.

**Effect of the finned Swordfish on the references, hashes and goldens (MS09b).** The 26 labelled reference puzzles keep every level and every `v3Expected` entry; three ratings change (`escargot` 5,279 to 5,143, `constructed-nightmare-1` 2,727 to 2,743 and `constructed-nightmare-2` 2,652 to 2,633, the finned Swordfish being used twice in each; levels and hardest techniques unchanged). Of the 520 regression hashes 13 change, none below Hard (`classic9/expert` seeds 34, 55, 233 and 10,946; `classic9/master` seeds 610 and 1,597; `classic9/nightmare` seed 13; `diagonalX/nightmare` seeds 1, 21 and 610; `jigsaw9/master` seeds 1, 8 and 6,765), none below Hard. Of the five replay wires only G11 (Greater-Than Expert 42) changes, in two counters of its local search (`work[4].localSearches[0]`: `firstShallowAt` null to 0 and `shallowRatings` 0 to 1); of the 14 analyses G07, G12 and G14 change (11, 1 and 9 fields: G07's end position at 31 is 2,268 instead of 2,310, G12's at 103 is 2,701 instead of 2,686) and the example of the Generation Steps guides (G01) is unchanged; the pins of the replay and steps suites move with them, like for like. The `nishioDeep` negative fixture of the technique ladder is now `V2_PUZZLE_1` (a position that the depth-1 chain refutes, with the null deep result at the same prefix) instead of ESCARGOT, whose depth-1 refutation no longer exists once the finned Swordfish precedes the chains. Of the help examples `finnedSwordfish` is new (Nightmare seed 3, step 18: digit 8, base lines R5, R7 and R8, covers C1, C5 and C7, the fins R5C8 and R5C9, R6C7 loses the 8) and `simpleColouring` and `nishioDeep` are made again from their own sources, which only moves their step numbers (24 to 25 and 5 to 2).

**Remote pairs (rating v3, MS10a).** `remotePairs` (group `wing`, base cost 35, weight 350, unscoped, `assumesUniqueness: false`; registered at order 116, between `twoStringKite` (114, 340, 34) and `simpleColouring` (120, 360, 36): the placement decided for RP-01 as S-OD9, see **Reachability** below) names the chain of identical two-candidate cells. **Proof.** Take the open cells that hold exactly the same two candidates {*X*, *Y*}; a pair of digits held by fewer than four such cells has no path of three edges and costs nothing. Two of them that are real peers (`model.peers`: a shared row, column, box, region, diagonal, window, overlapped box or cage; a unit need not be complete, so the mates of a cage are peers, and no edge is added beyond `model.peers`, so an inequality adds none) must take different digits, that is *X* and *Y* in the one order or the other, so along a path of such cells the two digits alternate. The cells joined by such edges form a component, which is two-coloured by a breadth-first walk from its lowest cell. A component with an odd cycle (a peer edge between two cells of one colour, for example three cells of the pair in one unit) has no solution: the whole component is skipped, never a piece of it. In a consistent component two cells of opposite colours are joined by a path with an odd number of **edges** (cells do not count), 1 if they are peers and at least 3 otherwise. The ends of a path of three edges or more hold *X* and *Y*, one each, so between them they use both digits, and *X* and *Y* leave every open cell that sees both ends (real peers) and still has one of them; a target loses only the digits it still has, and it is never a cell of the component (it would be adjacent to both ends, which two opposite colours forbid), so the proving cells never lose a digit. **The rule of the ends (SA2, 2026-11-16):** the shortest path between the selected visible opposite-colour ends must have at least three edges; adjacent ends alone do not qualify; another adjacent-pair justification does not invalidate a genuine remote witness (a target that also sees an adjacent opposite-colour pair still loses both digits, because the two bivalue cells of that pair use both of them as well), and the chosen witness is not claimed to be the shortest among all the possible justifications of the target. Two cells of one colour, or of different components, prove nothing; two cells that see each other only along a diagonal or another extra unit are an adjacent pair, not a remote pair. The search takes the components by their lowest cell, then the lower end and the higher end (cell order), and returns the first pair of ends that has a target, with all its targets. Finding: `{ technique: 'remotePairs', cells (the cells of one shortest path between the two ends, ascending: a breadth-first search from the lower end over the peers in ascending order, the first discoverer being the parent; not the whole component), placements: [], eliminations (target cell ascending, then digit ascending, both digits where the target has them), i18nKey: 'technique.remotePairs.step', i18nArgs: { digits (the pair, ascending), ends (the two end labels, in cell order), length (the number of edges of that path: 3, 5, 7, ...) } }`; the path is a shortest one for the chosen ends, the arrays are the call's own and the state is only read. The vector of the brief (R1C1, R2C2, R2C5 and R5C5 hold {2, 7}; the edges are box 1, row 2 and column 5) has two targets, R1C5 and R5C1, and the Finding lists both; with R1C5 stripped of 2 and 7 the brief's single target R5C1 loses both (the tests pin the two states, the transposed vector, a chain of five edges, the shortest way round an 8-cycle, a tie in a 6-cycle, the 5-cycle and the triangle that reject their components, and a target that also sees an adjacent pair). It fires on every board, since only real peers count: the tests compare with an independent enumeration (built from `model.units` alone) on classic9 (two seeds), classic6, classic4, 12 and 16 digits, Diagonal, Hyper and Jigsaw populations, on constructed chains on those boards and on the four multi-grid boards, and on an assembled caged Killer model (below); a Killer board without cages, Greater-Than and Wordoku have the peers of classic9 (an inequality adds no peer), so only the census below measures them. **Work (R10):** one unit is one edge extension (a same-pair open peer found while a cell of a component is expanded, in the colouring walk and in the walk of the witness path) or one tested pair of ends (opposite colours, not peers); the limit is 50,000 per call (`SG.wingSearch.REMOTE_PAIRS_WORK_LIMIT`); the counter is local to the call, the parent budget is checked at entry and before the unit that would carry the work since the last check over 256 units, a spent limit ends the call with `null` and `capHit: 'work'` (a component is used only once its colouring is complete, so a lost deduction is never an invented one), a spent budget is "nothing found", cancellation and every other error propagate, the observer `ctx.options.onPatternWork` gets `{ id, work, limit, capHit (null | 'work'), retained (null) }` per call, and `SG.wingSearch.remotePairs(model, state, ctx, { workLimit })` is the unregistered seam of the tests (the vector costs 12 units: 6 to colour, 1 pair, 5 to walk the witness). **Structural maximum** (units per call: every open cell is expanded at most once, so at most the sum of the peers of all cells; plus the tested pairs of consistent components, at most *n* × *k* / 4 with *k* = 2 × units / (fewest units of a cell), since a unit holds at most two cells of a consistent component; plus one witness walk of at most twice the units): 168 on 4×4, 576 on 6×6, 2,038 on the Classic, Greater-Than and Wordoku boards and on a Killer board with no cages (2,052 Jigsaw, 2,170 Diagonal, 2,215 Hyper), 4,968 on the 12-digit and 12,128 on the 16-digit board, 6,120 on the Butterfly, 8,950 on the Flower, 11,592 on the Sohei and 16,384 on the Samurai (the largest of the uncaged boards, 33 % of the limit); a caged Killer board has more peers, since a cage is a partial unit and its mates are peers whatever their rows and columns: 2,268 for the 25-cage Killer puzzle of the help catalogue (52 units, 1,638 peers in all, nine cage-only peer edges), 2,274 for 27 three-cell box cages, at most 3,131 for any generated layout (cages of 2 to 7 cells) and at most 7,505 for an imported layout of any shape (every cell a peer of every other); the limit therefore binds on no registered board, caged or not, and is a safety bound, and the tests reach it with an explicit `workLimit`. **Observed** (one process, finder measurements, not GP or qualification verdicts): **7,168 sampled calls** on the agreement populations (classic9 seeds 99 and 777 with 1,600 states each and seed 20260101 with 300, classic6 600, classic4 400, 12 digits 160, 16 digits 12, Diagonal, Hyper, Jigsaw, Killer (the uncaged board of the test helper), Greater-Than and Wordoku 400 each, and Samurai, Sohei, Butterfly and Flower two seeds of 12 each) found a remote pair in 199 of them, none capped, the largest work 35 units and the slowest call 0.465 ms; **17 constructed calls** (every cell holds {1, 2}, one component with odd cycles) reach exactly the sum of the peers (112 on 4×4, 1,620 on classic9, 9,984 on the 16-digit board, 7,812 on the Samurai), none capped; the limit was not adjusted (`docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS10a/work-census.json`, `structural-max.txt`). **Caged Killer (R13).** Those populations used the Killer board of the test helper, which has no cages, so the cages were measured on their own (`killer-bound.txt`, `killer-bound.json`): on the Killer puzzle of the help catalogue, 46 positions of its solve trajectory and 400 pruned random states of its solution found nothing, none capped, the largest work 0 (the slowest call 0.35 ms), and a flood costs exactly its 1,638 peers (1,620 for the 27 box cages). A test assembles that puzzle with its 25 cages (`tech-wing.test.js`): R7C3 and R8C4 share the cage R7C2, R7C3, R7C4, R8C4 and no row, column or box, so they are peers of the caged model and not of the classic one; the chain R1C2, R2C3, R7C3, R8C4 of the digits 6 and 7 (its edges are box 1, column 3 and that cage edge) is a remote pair of three edges whose targets R1C4, R7C2 and R8C2 lose both digits, sound against the solution with no proving cell losing a digit, and the same cells on the uncaged board find nothing. **A cage-only peer edge invalidates the complete-unit subsumption proof; this alone does not demonstrate normal-hint reachability.** The test is evidence of the finder, not of the hint: no help example uses it.

**Reachability of the remote pairs (RP-01, S-OD9; S-AV32 stays open for MS18e).** The first registration, at order 132 and weight 440, behind Simple Colouring, never fired in a real solve: 0 of 30,000 positions and 0 of 20,000 solves of the seeded Classic 9×9 corpus (seed 4242). After the naked pairs are done, every Remote Pairs deduction on a standard board is also a Simple Colouring deduction, and the ladder names the simpler pattern first: on the complete-unit peer graph an exhausted Naked Pair makes each same-pair peer edge a strong link for both digits, so the colouring contains the remote-pair component and makes its own trap or wrap deduction, and the solver restarts the ladder after every deduction. The plan author's corrected prior, confirmed by the owner (S-OD9), therefore places it before Simple Colouring: order 116, weight 350, base cost 35, between the two-string kite and Simple Colouring; the proof, the Finding, the seam and the 50,000 limit are as above, and an additional visible adjacent pair does not invalidate a valid longer witness. Corpus seed 4242, original givens: the 30,000-puzzle scan of the positions below the cutoff (the order of `remotePairs` read from the registry, 116, with the 14 techniques before it) is fully solved by that prefix for 20,581 puzzles and meets a remote pair at 51 positions (0.170 % of the attempted); the 20,000-puzzle scan of complete solves (`assumeUniqueness: true`, the default ladder) solves all 20,000 and 45 of them contain a Remote Pairs step (0.2250 % of the attempted and of the solved; 46 steps; the hardest technique of 20 solves; the lowest are puzzles 254, 1758, 1810, 2202, 2250, 3916, 3928 and 4533). The pass condition was at least one completed solve with the step; the rate is the named item S-AV32 (`docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS10a/logs/explore-dug-4242-30000-order116.txt` and `explore-solves-4242-20000-order116.txt`; the four logs of the first placement are kept unchanged). The help example is the first of those eight puzzles that the Player's `game.hint()` reaches: `testkit-4242-254`, step 30, digits 2 and 9, ends R6C9 and R9C2, three edges, R9C9 loses the 9.

**Effect of the remote pairs on the references, hashes and goldens (MS10a).** The 26 labelled reference puzzles keep every score, level and `v3Expected` entry (none uses the technique: `rated-effect.txt`, no rating changed, with and without it; the cost of the finder inside a rating is below the noise, 0.350 against 0.348 s for the 26, `cost-attribution.txt`); **none of the 520 regression hashes changes** (the ten cells of Hard and above admit the technique, 200 keys, all unchanged). Of the wires only **G10** (Killer Nightmare 42) differs, in three counters of the local search of its first attempt (`work[0].ratings` 600 to 574, and `work[0].localSearches[0].ratings` and `.shallowRatings` 138 to 112): the same puzzle, the same digging path. Of the analyses only **G06** (Jigsaw 9×9 Nightmare 42) differs, in three fields at its position 30 (the score 3,002 to 3,017, in the details row and in the fold peak, and the choice maximum of its first jump 630 to 616): a Remote Pairs step takes the place of a Simple Colouring step there and one more X-Wing follows (`g06-cause.txt`). No pin of the replay, steps, player, pipeline, cache or worker suites changes (681 of 681 pass on the new goldens), and `rated.js` is untouched.

**WXYZ-Wing (rating v3, MS10b).** `wxyzWing` (group `wing`, base cost 57, weight 800, order 155, unscoped, `assumesUniqueness: false`; registered between `xyzWing` (150, 750) and `wWing` (170, 850)) names the four-cell pattern whose capacity is one cell short. It is a Master-tier technique by the weight rule alone: 1.25 × 800 = 1,000 is not below Expert's maximum (the floor prefix and the order-follows-weight rule hold). **Proof (R13).** Take four distinct open cells of one to four candidates each whose candidates together are exactly four digits. A digit of the four is *restricted* when every pair of its occurrences in the four cells are peers (`model.peers`: a shared row, column, box, region, diagonal, window, overlapped box or cage; a digit that occurs in one cell only is restricted too) and *non-restricted* when two of its occurrences are not peers; exactly one digit *z* is asked to be non-restricted. Every cell of the quartet takes one of the four digits. The other three digits are restricted, so each of them is taken by at most one cell (two peers never take the same digit), together by at most three cells: **at least one cell of the quartet takes *z***, and that cell holds *z* as a candidate. An open cell outside the quartet that sees **every** quartet cell holding *z* cannot be that cell and cannot hold *z* either, so *z* is removed from it; a cell that sees only some of them could be the one that holds it and keeps *z* (a target never is a cell of the quartet: a cell does not see itself, and a quartet cell without *z* has nothing to lose). Nothing else is asked, because the argument needs nothing else: no pivot cell, no connected or unit shape, no minimum of two candidates in a cell (SA2's ruling of the pre-E question 2, A; the XY-Wing, the XYZ-Wing and a naked quad are other patterns or leave no non-restricted digit, and fewer or more than four digits, no non-restricted digit or two of them prove nothing). A sampled scan of 3,000 positions of seed 4242 (the positions after the techniques below it, `scope-check-final.txt`) holds 352 positions with a pattern and 628 patterns, none with a single-candidate cell and none with a cell that shares no digit with the other three (an isolated cell, not a test of general connectivity): **sampled evidence, not an equivalence proof**. Finding: `{ technique: 'wxyzWing', cells (the four cells, ascending), placements: [], eliminations (target cell ascending, digit z), i18nKey: 'technique.wxyzWing.step', i18nArgs: { digits (the four digits, ascending, z included), digit (z), cells (the four labels, cell order) } }`. The quartets are searched depth first in ascending cell order (the lowest cell, then the next above it, and so on), a partial quartet is dropped when its union passes four digits or it holds a second non-restricted digit, and the first quartet with a target is returned with all its targets. The vector of the brief (R1C1 {1, 4}, R1C5 {1, 2}, R1C9 {2, 3}, R5C9 {3, 4}: only the 4 is non-restricted, R5C1 sees both cells that hold it and loses it) is pinned with its transpose, any four digits, a second non-restricted digit (rejected), a naked quad, five and three digits, a 4 added to the second cell (the target misses a *z* cell: nothing), three *z* cells, a quartet cell as target (never), solved cells, a naked single as an open quartet cell, cells of five candidates (never in a quartet), two quartets in one state (the first in cell order), an assembled caged Killer model (a cage-only peer edge: the same cells on the uncaged board find nothing), a diagonal edge and a layout twin on the Samurai (a layout row is no peer), and constructed vectors on every board; the tests compare the finder with an independent digit-set-first enumeration (`wxyzOf`), an exhaustive capacity check (`fillings`) and a recount of the work units (`wxyzUnits`).

**Work and truncation of the WXYZ-Wing (R10, MS10b).** One unit is one open cell of at most four candidates tested as the next member of a partial quartet, the first member of a quartet included; a cell that would raise the union above four digits or add a second non-restricted digit costs its unit and is not extended, and the target scan (one pass over the peers of one cell) belongs to the completing extension. The limit is **50,000 per call** (`SG.wingSearch.WXYZ_WORK_LIMIT`, its own constant; the seam `SG.wingSearch.wxyzWing(model, state, ctx, { workLimit })`, SA2's ruling of the pre-E question 3, A; no limit adjustment): the counter is local to the call, the parent budget is checked at entry and at most every 256 units, a spent budget is "nothing found" with `capHit: null`, cancellation and every other error propagate, and the observer gets `{ id: 'wxyzWing', work, limit, capHit (null | 'work'), retained: null }` per call. **A spent limit is a deterministic truncation:** the searched prefix is the first 50,000 extensions in the order above; the call returns `null` with `capHit: 'work'`, which never proves that no WXYZ-Wing exists, a Finding completed inside the prefix is valid as always, and the same state gives the same prefix (the vector costs 4 units: found at a limit of 4 and lost at 3; the 4×4 flood costs 583). **Structural bounds** (not observations): the crude bound is the number of sets of at most four pool cells, C(*n*, 1) + C(*n*, 2) + C(*n*, 3) + C(*n*, 4): 2,516 on 4×4, 66,711 on 6×6, 1,752,381 on every 9×9 board, 17,676,660 on the 12-digit board, 177,589,056 on the 16-digit board and 768,368,085 on the Samurai; the **flood** (every cell holds {1, 2, 3, 4}; it contains no WXYZ-Wing and, on the boards of more digits, is no valid solvable state) measures the exact cost of the prefix: 583 on 4×4, 7,587 on 6×6, 93,035 on classic9 (and on Greater-Than, Wordoku and the uncaged Killer model of the test helper), 94,491 Jigsaw, 99,425 Diagonal, 107,759 Hyper, 544,372 on 12 digits, more than 3,000,000 on 16 digits, 384,737 Butterfly, 720,443 Flower, 1,568,240 Sohei and 2,531,897 Samurai (`structural-max-final.txt`); the limit is not scaled to them. **Observed** (`work-census.js`, one process, finder measurements, not GP or qualification verdicts; the preliminary record `work-census-first.json` and the final one `work-census.json`, on the final source, agree): **7,168 sampled calls** (the agreement populations of the suite: classic9 seeds 99 and 777 with 1,600 states each and seed 20260101 with 300, classic6 600, classic4 400, 12 digits 160, 16 digits 12, Diagonal, Hyper, Jigsaw, Killer (the **uncaged** 27-unit model of the test helper, **not assembled-cage evidence**: a cage adds peers, and a model assembled with cages was not measured), Greater-Than and Wordoku 400 each, and the four multi-grid boards two seeds of 12 each) found a WXYZ-Wing in 3,848 of them and **two capped, both Samurai** (state 1 of seed 4242 and state 9 of seed 777, one of twelve each, 0.546 and 0.451 ms): a capped `null` is not a missed deduction; the **Sohei is 0 of 24 capped**, its largest work 42,542 (85 % of the limit), and the other largest works per cohort are 8,696 on classic9 (p95 0.054 ms, the slowest call of the census 1.347 ms), 26,440 on the 16-digit board (53 %), 11,125 on 12 digits, 6,633 Diagonal, 5,893 Butterfly and 4,684 Flower. **17 constructed calls** (the floods, one per board) are kept apart: **fifteen capped** (every board of nine digits or more), classic4 and classic6 not; the floods contain no WXYZ-Wing, so those cap hits are the cost of the bounded prefix, neither a lost deduction nor a cap rate of real puzzles (0.83 to 0.97 ms for 50,000 units). **Named item S-AV33, "WXYZ-Wing bounded-prefix coverage/cost"**, goes to MS18d and the MS18e audit with this evidence (the capped-state indices, the populations and the latency); the limit was not adjusted.

**Reachability of the WXYZ-Wing (seed 4242).** Corpus seed 4242, original givens: of 30,000 dug puzzles the 23 techniques below it leave 7,320 unsolved and **3,522 positions (11.74 % of the attempted, 48 % of those) hold a WXYZ-Wing**; of 20,000 complete solves (`assumeUniqueness: true`, the default ladder) **3,323 use it (16.615 %)**, and it is the hardest technique of 1,057 (`explore-dug-4242-30000.txt`, `explore-solves-4242-20000.txt`). No placement question arose (unlike the remote pairs): the rates are those of a technique that the ladder reaches before the wings above it and the chains; they are not a gate. The help example is a normal hint of Nightmare 13 (one of the existing wing and chain sources of the catalogue), step 37: digits 1, 4, 7 and 9, *z* = 9, cells R2C7, R3C8, R4C7 and R6C7, R6C8 loses the 9.

**Effect of the WXYZ-Wing on the references, hashes and goldens (MS10b).** R20 authorizes the reclassification that follows from a correct computation; the historical v2 and external labels are untouched. Of the 26 labelled references **four change score and none changes level, requiredness (at the stated v3 level), hardest technique or `v3Expected` entry** (`golden-nugget` 5,689 to 5,681, `constructed-nightmare-1` 2,743 to 2,739, `-2` 2,633 to 2,661 and `-3` 2,652 to 2,565, the technique being used 1, 4, 1 and 3 times: `rated-effect.txt`). Of the 520 regression hashes **36 change, all in the four cells whose level admits the technique** (`classic9/master` 13 seeds, `classic9/nightmare` 6, `diagonalX/nightmare` 7 and `jigsaw9/master` 10); the other 44 of their 80 keys and the 440 keys of the 22 cells below Master are unchanged. The cause is shown by regenerating the 80 admitting keys with and without the technique in one process (`hash-cause.txt`): the same 36 differ, and without the technique every hash equals the previous record, because the digger rates its candidate puzzles with the registered ladder, a WXYZ-Wing step takes the place of a heavier step in some of them, and so a different candidate is accepted or refused at those levels. Of the five replay wires **G10** (Killer Nightmare 42, 23 fields: the digging path changes at its 229th log entry, the puzzle differs in six givens, the score 2,051 to 2,199) and **G13** (Samurai Nightmare 42, 1,066 fields: another dig path, 123 instead of 133 positions) change; G09, G11 and G12 match. Of the 14 analyses **nine change** (G03 8 fields, G04 21, G05 11, G06 19, G07 2, G10 29, G12 5, G13 60 and G14 12; G01, which the guides show, G02, G08, G09 and G11 match), each by a WXYZ-Wing step that takes the place of a forcing-chain, nishio or W-Wing step in the same puzzle (G03, G05, G06, G07, G12, G14: `g-cause.txt`; G06's position 29 now has the WXYZ-Wing as its hardest technique, Nightmare to Master) or by another dig path (G04, G10, G13). The pins that follow are in `evidence.md` section 6; the G13 and G04 pins that no longer hold (a flat new hardest technique, an unfinished check, one exchange) are read from the goldens that now have them, as listed there, and three puzzles of the long-chain list of `tech-chain` (§15.2) are replaced in place.

**Unique Rectangle types 3 and 4 (rating v3, MS11a).** `uniqueRectangle` (group `wing`, order 180, base cost 66, weight 900, unscoped, `assumesUniqueness: true`, all unchanged) now names four subtypes under one ID, with the step keys `technique.uniqueRectangle.type1.step` to `.type4.step`; the catalogue is still 35 techniques with one `uniqueRectangle`. **Preconditions, unchanged.** The rectangles are those of `buildRectangles` / `isDeadlyGeometry` (two rows, two columns and two box-like units touch the four cells, and every unit that touches them holds exactly two of them, so that swapping two digits keeps every unit valid), a model with cages or inequalities has none, and `ctx.assumeUniqueness: false` gives `null`. **The premise (R14).** Two corners, the *floors*, hold exactly {*X*, *Y*}; the other two, the *roofs*, hold proper supersets of it. If both roofs took a value from {*X*, *Y*}, the four corners would hold only those two digits and swapping them would give a second solution; so in a puzzle with one solution **at least one roof holds one of the extras *E*** (the candidates of the roofs outside {*X*, *Y*}). It is a statement about one roof: *E* is one virtual member of a subset, never a fixed extra digit in both roofs. **Type 3, the extras as one virtual subset member.** Take a unit that holds both roofs (any unit of the model whatever its kind, because the argument needs only that its cells hold different digits; the roofs need not be equal and need not share a side, only a unit) and *k* other open cells of it, the *real cells*, whose candidates together with *E* are exactly *k* + 1 digits *D*; there is no cap on *k*: it runs from max(1, |*E*| − 1) to the number of eligible cells (the open cells of the unit outside the rectangle), at most *digits* − 1, because |*D*| = *k* + 1 cannot pass the digits of the board. The roof that holds an extra and the *k* real cells are *k* + 1 different cells of the unit whose digits are all in *D*, so together they hold all of *D*, and every other open cell of the unit, the two roofs and the real cells aside, loses the digits of *D* that it still has (the roofs keep theirs: one of them is a member). **Type 4, a conjugate link between the roofs.** Take a **complete** unit that holds both roofs (a digit occurs in it exactly once, `ctx.completeUnits`; a partial unit proves nothing) in which a digit *L* of the pair is a candidate of exactly the two roofs (a solved cell that holds *L* counts as an occurrence): exactly one roof is *L*, and the other roof cannot be the other digit of the pair either, or all four corners would hold only {*X*, *Y*}; so that other digit leaves both roofs. **Order.** The first pass (types 1 and 2, rectangle by rectangle, as before) runs over every rectangle first; only if it finds nothing does the second pass run, rectangle by rectangle in the same order, and on each rectangle **type 3 in every unit that holds both roofs (units in ascending index, subset sizes from max(1, |*E*| − 1) upward, the real cells in ascending cell order) before type 4** (units in ascending index, the digit *L* low to high); the first Finding is returned, so an old type keeps its priority over every new subtype, also on a later rectangle, and a size below |*E*| − 1 cannot reach *k* + 1 digits and is not tried. **No cap on the real cells (SA2, R14).** The first source of this task stopped at three real cells (the largest naked subset); R14 states the virtual-subset rule without such a limit, so that source missed deductions: with the extras {3,4} in the roofs R2C1 {1,2,3} and R2C4 {1,2,4} and the real cells R2C3 {4,7}, R2C7 {3,5}, R2C8 {5,6} and R2C9 {6,7} (the vector of the brief), the roof and four real cells hold the five digits {3,4,5,6,7}, and these leave R2C2, R2C5 and R2C6; on a 12-digit board the same chain with five real cells (D = {3,...,8}) and six (D = {3,...,9}) is found too. A real cell can belong to a subset only if its candidates with *E* are at most min(eligible cells, *digits* − 1) + 1 digits (|*D*| = *k* + 1 and *k* is at most that minimum); this union-size filter and the bound |*D*| = *k* + 1 at the leaf are the only pruning, and the work limit below is the only bound on the search. **Findings.** Type 3: `{ technique: 'uniqueRectangle', cells (the four rectangle cells in rectangle order, then the real cells ascending), placements: [], eliminations (cell ascending, digit ascending), i18nKey: 'technique.uniqueRectangle.type3.step', i18nArgs: { roof1, roof2 (labels, lower cell first), digits ([X, Y]), partDigits (D ascending), cells (the labels of the real cells, cell order), unit (its label) } }`. Type 4: `{ ..., cells (the four rectangle cells), eliminations ([roof1, digit], [roof2, digit]), i18nKey: 'technique.uniqueRectangle.type4.step', i18nArgs: { roof1, roof2, digits ([X, Y]), digit (the removed digit), unit } }`; the arguments reuse the localized names `digits`, `digit` and `partDigits` of the Player (`localizeArgs`), and `unit` also gives the Player the region of the hint; the type 3 text says that *D* leaves the cells of the unit outside **both** roofs and the real cells, because the roof that holds the extra digit is not identified. **Where it can fire.** Every board without cages and inequalities that has box-like units: the 9×9 boards (a Jigsaw region is a box-like unit and a unit of the roofs), the 4, 6, 12 and 16 digit boards, Sudoku X and Hyper when every extra unit holds two or none of the four corners, and the Samurai, the Sohei and the Flower, where only a unit of the model holds both roofs (a layout row is not one: a real cell of another grid in the same layout row is tested as a negative); the Butterfly has no rectangle. **Tests.** The brief's vectors (floors R1C1 and R1C4 {1,2}, roofs R2C1 {1,2,3} and R2C4 {1,2,4}: the real cell R2C7 {3,4} removes 3 and 4 from the rest of row 2, and 1 only in the two roofs of row 2 removes 2 from both) with their transposes; subsets of one to six real cells (four real cells on nine digits in 24 units, five and six on twelve digits in 41 and 77 units, each with its capped prefix), with three, four and five extras, and the exact union (a real cell inside the extras alone, in a partial unit, is no type 3); empty extras, wrong union sizes, a real cell outside the unit, an open cell without candidates, the roofs as real cells (never), solved cells; roofs on opposite corners (no common unit) and one floor of three digits; a third occurrence breaking the conjugacy, a solved occurrence; a partial unit (type 3 works in it, type 4 does not, and the same unit complete is a link), an extra unit that holds one corner (nothing), cages and signs (nothing), the uniqueness context (nothing, and no record); the old types first, also on a later rectangle, type 3 before type 4, unit order, subset order, rectangle order, a unit listed in descending order, a Jigsaw region and constructed vectors on the boards of 4, 6, 9, 12 and 16 digits; the finder is compared field by field with an independent brute-force enumeration (`urSubtypesOf`: layout positions and `model.units` only, every subset tried, no pool) on 1,800 seeded real states and a recount of the work units (`urUnitsOf`), and each real position shows the premise absurd (with both roofs forced into {*X*, *Y*} the unique puzzle has no completion).

**Work and truncation of the Unique Rectangle types 3 and 4 (R10, MS11a).** One unit is one tested extension of a type 3 subset (a cell of the unit that could belong to one, the first cell included, tried as the next real cell; a cell that raises the union above *k* + 1 digits costs its unit and is not extended; the pool is the open cells of the unit outside the rectangle whose candidates with *E* are at most min(eligible cells, *digits* − 1) + 1 digits) or one tested digit of a type 4 link; collecting the pool, the list of eliminations and the Finding belong to the unit that completes them. The first pass is unbounded and unchanged. The limit is **50,000 per call** (`SG.wingSearch.UNIQUE_RECTANGLE_WORK_LIMIT`, its own constant; the seam `SG.wingSearch.uniqueRectangle(model, state, ctx, { workLimit })`; no adjustment): the counter is local to the call, the parent budget is checked at the entry of the second pass and at most every 256 units, a spent budget is "nothing found" with `capHit: null`, cancellation and every other error propagate, and the observer gets `{ id: 'uniqueRectangle', work, limit, capHit (null | 'work'), retained: null }` for every call that runs the second pass (none for a Finding of the first pass or for `assumeUniqueness: false`). **A spent limit is a deterministic truncation:** `null` with `capHit: 'work'` proves no absence, and a Finding completed inside the prefix is valid (a witness three units deep is found at a limit of 3 and lost at 2; the five-real-cell witness of a 12-digit board is found at 41 and lost at 40, the six-real-cell one at 77 and 76). **Structural bound** (an upper bound, not an observation): per rectangle and choice of roofs the whole depth-first enumeration of 1 to min(*m*, *digits* − 1) real cells with nothing pruned (247 units for a unit of *m* = 7 other cells, 2,036 for 10, 32,752 for 14) plus two tested digits for a complete unit, at most two units hold both roofs (three on Hyper), summed over the rectangles: 192 on 4×4, 5,544 on 6×6, 242,028 on every 9×9 board of 486 rectangles, 74,700 Diagonal, 114,540 Hyper, 121,014 Jigsaw, 161,352 Flower, 591,624 Sohei, 833,652 Samurai, 6,309,648 on 12 digits and 301,860,864 on 16 digits (`structural-max.txt`; a model with cages or inequalities, and the Butterfly, has no rectangle); no state makes every rectangle spend its worst bound together, so these totals are far above anything reachable (the first, capped source had 88,452 on a 9×9 board, `structural-max-cap3.txt`) and the limit is not scaled to them. **Constructed cost:** one row of a 16-digit board whose other fourteen cells hold the pairs of a cycle over the digits {1,2,5,...,16} (no subset has *k* + 1 digits with the extras 3 and 4) spends 5,832 units and finds nothing, three such rows 17,532. **Observed** (`work-census.js`, one process, finder measurements, not GP or qualification verdicts; the populations are those of the agreement setups of the suite, the Killer and Greater-Than ones the uncaged models of the test helper), on the corrected source: **7,168 sampled calls, 6,842 of them ran the second pass, none capped**; the first Findings were 218 type 1, 108 type 2, 191 type 3 and 205 type 4, the largest work was 186 units (classic9, seed 777; 163 and 142 on the other classic9 populations, 232 on 12 digits, 122 on the Sohei, 120 on Wordoku, 140 on Hyper, 93 on the uncaged Killer, 85 on Jigsaw, 53 on 16 digits, 52 on Diagonal, 50 on 6×6, 39 on the Samurai, 28 on the Flower, 10 on 4×4) and the mean call costs 0.019 to 0.028 ms on classic9 against 0.010 to 0.014 ms for the first pass alone (the base commit's engine, `work-census-base.json`; the first, capped source measured 0.019 to 0.026 ms and a largest work of 52, `work-census-cap3.txt`); the work census and the structural bound are evidence for the owner, not a gate (`pattern-cost.json`: the four positives and two negatives of the fixtures, 120 finds, work 2, 23, 67 and 0 units, none capped, p95 at most 0.4 ms). No sampled call hit the limit, so no cap item is opened here; the limit was not adjusted.

**Reachability of the Unique Rectangle types 3 and 4 (seed 4242).** Corpus seed 4242, original givens: of 30,000 dug puzzles the 25 techniques below it leave 5,118 unsolved, and at that position **1,025 puzzles (20.0 % of the open ones, 3.4 % of the attempted) have a Unique Rectangle as the first deduction: 452 type 1, 95 type 2, 146 type 3 and 332 type 4**, so the new types 3 and 4 are (146 + 332) / 5,118 = **9.34 %** of the open positions and the 20.0 % includes all four types; these figures were measured on the first source, which stopped at three real cells, before SA2 lifted that cap, and were not repeated on the corrected search (no new campaign was required; the census above is the corrected measurement); no call was capped and no Finding contradicted the solution (`explore-dug-4242-30000.txt`). Of 20,000 complete solves (`assumeUniqueness: true`, the default ladder) **152 use a type 3 step (0.76 %) and 321 a type 4 step (1.605 %)**, 377 a type 1 and 81 a type 2, and the hardest technique of 231 is the Unique Rectangle (`explore-solves-4242-20000.txt`). No placement question arose: the technique keeps its order and weight, so these rates are not a gate.

**Effect of the Unique Rectangle types 3 and 4 on the references, hashes and goldens (MS11a).** R20 authorizes the reclassification that follows from a correct computation; the historical v2 and external labels are untouched. Of the 26 labelled references **none changes score, level, requiredness, hardest technique or `v3Expected` entry** (`rated-effect.txt`: each is rated with the registered ladder and with a finder that returns nothing where it would return a type 3 or a type 4; `escargot` has one type 2 use and no type 3 or 4). Of the 520 regression hashes **3 change, all in `classic9/nightmare`** (seeds 3, 13 and 233), one of the four cells whose level admits the technique; the other 17 keys of that cell, the 60 keys of `classic9/master`, `diagonalX/nightmare` and `jigsaw9/master`, and the 440 keys of the 22 cells below Master are unchanged. The cause is shown by regenerating the 80 admitting keys with and without types 3 and 4 in one process (`hash-cause.txt`): the same 3 differ, and without them every hash equals the previous record, because the digger rates its candidate puzzles with the registered ladder, a type 3 or type 4 step takes the place of a heavier step in some of them, and so a different candidate is accepted or refused. All five replay wires (G09 to G13) match the published ones. Of the 14 analyses **one changes: G14** (Mega 16 Nightmare, seed 12; 12 fields): the puzzle is the same with and without the types, and a Unique Rectangle step takes the place of a heavier step at 5 of its 78 positions (positions 70, 71, 72, 73 and 77: scores 1,766 to 1,887, 2,635 to 2,666, 3,675 to 3,688, 3,894 to 3,904 and 6,473 to 6,479; at position 72 a box-line step is replaced; `g-cause.txt`); G01, which the guides show, and the other twelve match. No pin beyond these fixtures moved in the Generator (681 of 681 after the goldens). Two of the 35 help examples were affected: Remote Pairs no longer has a hint of its own on its source and Long Forcing Chain moved by two steps on its own source (`player/design.md`, "Difficulty v3, MS11a"). The corrected search (no cap on the real cells, SA2's ruling) is the one recorded here; the first source of this task, capped at three real cells, gave the same references and the same G14 change in the preview before E (`logs/g-cause-preview-g14.txt`, `rated-effect-cap3.txt`) and is kept only as preliminary history (`*-cap3*` files).

**Unique Rectangle, the hidden form (rating v3, MS11b).** `uniqueRectangle` (order 180, base cost 66, weight 900, unscoped, `assumesUniqueness: true`, all unchanged; one ID in the catalogue of 35) gets a fifth subtype, with the step key `technique.uniqueRectangle.hidden.step`; its types 1 to 4 and their order are unchanged. **Preconditions, unchanged.** The same rectangles (`buildRectangles` / `isDeadlyGeometry`: every unit that touches the four cells holds exactly two of them, so that swapping two digits keeps every unit valid), four open corners whose masks have **exactly two digits {*X*, *Y*} in common** (as for every other subtype), no cages and no inequalities, and `ctx.assumeUniqueness: false` gives `null`. A rectangle whose four corners all hold exactly {*X*, *Y*} is the deadly pattern itself (a position with two solutions) and has no hidden form; the existing regression "finds nothing in the bare deadly pattern of a puzzle with two solutions" stays. Neighbours that lack *Y* are not admitted. **The proof (R14).** One corner, the *floor* F, holds exactly {*X*, *Y*}; the *target* T is the corner opposite to it (it holds *X* and *Y* and anything else, or exactly the pair); P is the corner that shares a row unit with T and Q the corner that shares a column unit with T. If *X* is held by exactly two cells of the row unit of T and P, T and P, and by exactly two cells of the column unit of T and Q, T and Q (**conjugate links**; the units are the actual row and column, both **complete**, a solved cell that holds *X* counts as a holder, and a box or an extra unit that holds the same two cells is never a link), then T cannot be *Y*: P and Q would be *X* by the links, F sees both of them (it shares a column with P and a row with Q) and would be *Y*, so the four corners would hold *Y*, *X*, *X*, *Y* in a rectangle whose every unit holds two of them, and swapping the two digits would give a second solution; the puzzle has one. (The holders of *X* in a unit are counted and compared with two by equality; the two distinct corners of the pattern in that unit both hold *X*, so the count is never below two and a test of at most two would be equivalent: the mutation set records it as its one equivalent survivor.) So *Y* is removed from T, which holds it by construction (every pattern removes a candidate; a target that already holds exactly {*X*, *Y*} is left with *X*, and 128 of the 992 first hidden deductions of the corpus below are of this kind, SA2's ruling (a), the proof needs no extra candidate). A transposed rectangle needs no search of its own (the row link and the column link of T are symmetric: the four corners as target cover it), and the two digits are tried in both roles. **Order (SA2).** The first pass (types 1 and 2) and then the second pass (types 3 and 4, complete, rectangle by rectangle) keep their priority; only when both found nothing does a **third pass** run, over the rectangles in the order of the first pass, in a rectangle over the corners as target in the order of the rectangle (the corner opposite to it must hold exactly the pair and both of its units must be complete) and over the digit of the links, the lower one of the pair first; the first (target, digit) with both links is returned. A second pass that spent its own work limit does not stop the third pass (the limits are independent) and proves no absence of types 3 and 4; a spent parent budget does stop it. **Finding.** `{ technique: 'uniqueRectangle', cells (the four rectangle cells in rectangle order), placements: [], eliminations: [[target, Y]], i18nKey: 'technique.uniqueRectangle.hidden.step', i18nArgs: { digit (the digit *X* of the links: for this key only, the kept digit, not the removed one), digits ([*X*, *Y*] ascending even when *X* is the larger), cells (the labels of the four corners in rectangle order) } }`; the text (both apps, EN and VI) says that at least one corner holds only the pair, that in the row and in the column of the opposite corner *X* has just two places, that the digit of the pair other than *X* would force the swappable rectangle, and that it is removed from the opposite corner. **Where it can fire.** The same boards as the other subtypes (the 9×9 boards including Jigsaw, the 4, 6, 12 and 16 digit boards, Sudoku X and Hyper when every extra unit holds two or none of the four corners, the Samurai, the Sohei and the Flower in one grid; never the Butterfly, cages or signs). **Tests.** The vector of the brief (A = R1C1 {1,2}, B = R1C4 {1,2,4}, C = R2C1 {1,2,5}, D = R2C4 {1,2,3}; the 1 conjugate between D and C in row 2 and between D and B in column 4 removes 2 from D) with the swapped roles, both digits as links, the transposed vector and every corner as target, on the boards of 4, 6, 9, 12 and 16 digits, a 4×4 Jigsaw (a conjugate pair in a region is no link), Sudoku X and an extra unit of two corners, a Samurai first grid, and extra units listed before the rows (neither a row nor a column: no link); the near misses: an extra 1 (or a solved 1) in the row or in the column, only one link, links around a corner whose opposite corner is not the pair, a floor of three digits, four bare corners, one or three common digits, a corner without a digit of the pair, a solved corner (also when only its value is set), a pair confined in a box, a partial row or column, cages, signs and the uniqueness context; the old types first (also on a later rectangle), rectangle order, target order, digit order; the finder against an independent brute-force enumeration (`urHiddenTests`: layout positions and `model.units` only) on 1,800 seeded real states with minimum firings, a recount of the work units, the constructed floods, the exact work, limit, budget, cancellation and observer rules (below), and five real unique positions (puzzles 138, 272 and 244 of the seed 4242 corpus: the Finding of 138 is exact, the pattern it would force has no completion, and the reduced position still has the one solution).

**Work, records and truncation of the hidden form (R10, MS11b).** One unit is one tested (target, digit of the links), with the scans of its two units inside it; a target whose opposite corner does not hold exactly the pair, or whose row or column unit is partial, costs nothing, so a rectangle has at most 4 targets × 2 digits = **8 units** and a call at most 8 × (the rectangles of the model): **36,864 on 16 digits, 13,392 on the Samurai, 12,384 on 12 digits, 9,504 on the Sohei, 3,888 on every 9×9 board of 486 rectangles, 2,592 Flower, 1,944 Jigsaw, 1,520 Hyper, 1,200 Diagonal, 792 on 6×6, 128 on 4×4 and 0 on the Butterfly** (`structural-max.txt`; the Killer and Greater-Than rows are the uncaged / uninequalitied models of the test helper, real cages and signs exclude the rectangles). The limit is **50,000 per call** with a counter and a parent-budget cadence of its own (checked at the entry of the pass and at most every 256 units; `SG.wingSearch.UNIQUE_RECTANGLE_HIDDEN_WORK_LIMIT`, the seam `SG.wingSearch.uniqueRectangle(model, state, ctx, { workLimit, hiddenWorkLimit })`; no adjustment): the supplied bounds show that **this pass's** limit cannot bind on these geometries, not that the whole call, its latency or the parent budget are bounded by them; a spent limit is `null` with `capHit: 'work'` (a deterministic truncation: the vector, 25 units, is found at a limit of 25 and lost at 24), a spent parent budget is `null` with `capHit: null` and the caller's budget stays spent, cancellation and every other error propagate. **Records.** `ctx.options.onPatternWork` gets `{ id: 'uniqueRectangle', work, limit, capHit, retained: null }` once per bounded pass that ran, the second pass first: **no record** when the first pass finds something or the context rejects, **one** when the second pass returns a Finding or spends the parent budget, **two** when the hidden pass ran; each record keeps its own work and limit (a sum above 50,000 is not a single-pass overrun), and the records are published only after the last pass needed by the call returned normally, so **cancellation or an unexpected error in any pass leaves no record** (IR72 C2; an error that the observer itself throws propagates). `pattern-cost.js` keeps the ordered array and sums the work (`recordOf`), so a case of two same-ID records needs no tool change. **Observed on the final source** (`work-census.txt`, `census-totals.txt`; the populations and the order of the checks are those of MS11a's census, the base being the wing engine of `ef95fc5`, `work-census-base.txt`): of **7,168 sampled calls, 6,842 ran the second pass and 6,446 the hidden pass; 438 first Findings are hidden forms** (218 type 1, 108 type 2, 191 type 3 and 205 type 4 as at the base, since the hidden pass runs last); **no pass was capped**, the largest hidden work is **86** units (Samurai, against 232 for the second pass on 12 digits), and the whole-call latency of a mean call on classic9 is 0.024 to 0.033 ms with the hidden pass against 0.018 to 0.026 ms without it (a census in a locked but not protected process, not a promise); every record is the id `uniqueRectangle` and the order is second pass then hidden pass. Pattern cost (`pattern-cost.json`, 7 cases of 20 runs): work 2 and 23 on the two positives that reach the second pass, no report on the two that the first pass finds, two reports on the hidden positive and on each of the two negatives (their summed work, 93 and 18, is never compared with one limit; the tool prints "of 2 reports"), none capped, p95 at most 0.48 ms.

**Reachability of the Unique Rectangle hidden form (seed 4242).** Same corpus as above (30,000 dug puzzles, original givens, the 5,118 positions that stay open below the rectangle; `logs/explore-dug-4242-30000-final.txt`, measured on the pre-ruling source and not repeated): at that position **the hidden form is the first Unique Rectangle deduction of 992 (19.4 %)**, against 452 type 1, 95 type 2, 158 type 3 and 322 type 4 (1,027 of the older types; type 3 and 4 differ from the first-source figures above because the cap was lifted); 864 of the 992 have one corner that holds exactly the pair and 128 two (the floor and the target); no call was capped and every elimination agrees with the unique solution.

**Effect of the Unique Rectangle hidden form on the references, hashes and goldens (MS11b).** R20 authorizes the reclassification that follows from a correct computation; the historical v2 and external labels are untouched. Of the 26 labelled references **none changes level, requiredness, hardest technique or `v3Expected` entry** and **3 change score** (`rated-effect.txt`, each rated with the registered ladder and with a finder that returns nothing where it would return a hidden form): `escargot` 5,143 to 5,506 (its one Unique Rectangle use is a hidden form with the pass; the first task recorded one type 2 use without it, and the rating trajectories differ), `platinum-blonde` 5,675 to 5,688 and `constructed-nightmare-3` 2,565 to 2,586 (one hidden use each, where there was none); the other 23 are the same. Of the 520 regression hashes **5 change: `classic9/nightmare` seeds 8, 13, 21 and 610 and `diagonalX/nightmare` seed 21**, two of the four cells whose level admits the technique; the other 16 keys of `classic9/nightmare`, the 19 of `diagonalX/nightmare`, the 40 keys of `classic9/master` and `jigsaw9/master`, and the 440 keys of the 22 cells below Master are unchanged. The cause is shown by regenerating the 80 admitting keys with and without the hidden form in one process (`hash-cause.txt`): the same 5 differ and none is inconsistent, and without the form every hash equals the previous record, because the digger rates its candidate puzzles with the registered ladder, a hidden step takes the place of a heavier step in some of them, and so a different candidate is accepted or refused. All five replay wires (G09 to G13) match the published ones. Of the 14 analyses **one changes: G14** (Mega 16, level `hell2`, seed 12; the recorder reports 34 fields): the generated puzzle itself differs (80 rating positions instead of 78, 81 detail rows instead of 79) for the same reason, the rating positions 69 to 77 differ in score and in technique counts, and `g-cause-saved.txt` shows that the golden regenerated without the hidden form equals the saved one in every field while the other eleven goldens equal it with and without; `g-cause.txt` lists the differing positions. No other pin moved except what follows from these records and is listed with its before and after values in `docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS11b/evidence.md`: the stuck-board counts and the G14 witness move of `replay`, the shallow-fallback specimen of `cache`, `worker` and `pipeline` (classic9 Nightmare seed 6, the lowest shallow seed of a recorded scan, for 21), and the Hell 1 seed of one real pass of `digger-target` (14 for 11, so that its stuck-proposal coverage of more than 30 holds). One of the 35 help examples was affected: Empty Rectangle (the same Hell 1 seed 214 position and the same hint, now step 24 of its own source instead of 23; `player/design.md`, "Difficulty v3, MS11b"). The reach, the work and the effect above are those of the final source, except the reach campaign, which was measured on the source before SA2's record-publication correction (no Finding changes with it) and is not repeated.

**BUG+1 (rating v3, MS11c).** `bugPlusOne` (group `wing`, base cost 72, weight 920, order 182, between the Unique Rectangle and the Forcing Chain; unscoped, `assumesUniqueness: true`) places the one cell that a bivalue universal grave leaves with three candidates. **Preconditions.** Exactly one open cell T with three candidates and every other open cell with exactly two (a guard that costs no work), a model with **no cages and no inequalities and only complete units** (`ctx.completeUnits`; one partial unit refuses the whole model, since a partial unit has no digit count to compare), and `ctx.assumeUniqueness: false` gives `null` and no record. **The proof (R14).** Take X among the candidates of T, in ascending order. If, **with X left out of T, every unit of the model** (all of them, not only the units of T, and the units of every extra region, grid or diagonal) holds each placed digit in its own solved cell only and every other digit in exactly two cells, then every open cell of the position without X is bivalue and, in a solution, a digit that is not placed in a unit sits in exactly one of its two holders while the other holder holds its other digit; giving every open cell its other candidate is again a solution (in a unit the digit that leaves one holder enters the other holder and no other cell), and the two differ in every open cell. The position with X removed from T therefore has no solution or at least two (the global flip proves **at least two**, not exactly two: disconnected components can allow more); a unique puzzle has one solution, and it has T = X. At most one X passes (every other candidate of T has one holder fewer in some unit than its own removal needs), and the first is placed. **The guards that the proof needs.** A digit X that is already placed in a unit of T is a stale candidate: removing it would hide the placed holder, so such an X never qualifies (a soundness guard, regression-tested; a digit that is placed elsewhere, outside every unit of T, is no reason to refuse it); the exact count of candidates, the exclusion of every cage and sign, the check of every unit and the rejection of a false uniqueness context are kept. **Finding.** `{ technique: 'bugPlusOne', cells: [T], placements: [[T, X]], eliminations: [], i18nKey: 'technique.bugPlusOne.step', i18nArgs: { cell: T, label: cellLabel(T), digit: X } }`; the text (both apps, EN and VI) says that every other open cell has two candidates, that the three-candidate cell with X removed would leave every unit with two places for each digit, that "such a position has no solution or at least two solutions", and that, the puzzle having one solution, the cell takes X. **Where it can fire.** The boards without cages or inequalities (all 9×9 boards including Jigsaw, Sudoku X, Hyper and Wordoku; 4, 6, 12 and 16 digit boards; the Samurai, the Sohei, the Butterfly and the Flower, where a cell of two grids is in the units of both); never Killer or Greater-Than with their real cages and signs. **Tests.** The registration, the vector of the brief (every open cell two candidates but R5C5, whose third candidate is the answer in every unit) with the lowest, a middle and the highest candidate as the answer, a distant unit with a wrong count, a second three-candidate cell, a cell of four candidates, a naked single and an empty cell, the third candidate in another cell, the placed digits (all nine solved, a stale candidate, a second placement), an added diagonal, a partial unit, cages and signs, the uniqueness context; the unit shapes of every board of 4 to 16 digits and the multi-grid layouts; two real positives (puzzles 14 and 1,814 of the seed 4242 corpus, with an independent completion) and two negatives; the mutation set of 67 in-memory mutants of the finder (62 detected, 5 equivalent: the comparison of a count with its required value as "less than", as "greater than" or "or three", and the first or the last digit of a unit left unchecked, behave exactly like the equality, because in a complete unit of N cells with S fixed cells and P distinct fixed digits the tallied sum is 2N − S and the required sum 2N − P, never smaller, so each of the one-sided or partial checks still forces every count to its required value, rejects duplicated fixed values and stale holders, and rejects the same units at the same tally positions; a random search of 850,557 states with no disagreement corroborates the argument but is not a proof); the finder against an independent enumeration (`bugOf`: layout positions and `model.units` only) on seeded graves; and the exact work, limit, budget, cancellation and observer rules.

**Work, records and truncation of BUG+1 (R10, MS11c).** One unit is one cell tallied under one proposed digit in one unit of the model (fixed cells included), so a call costs at most 3 × the sum of the unit sizes: **729 on every classic 9×9 board, 783 Diagonal, 837 Hyper, 324 on 6×6, 144 on 4×4, 1,296 on 12 digits, 2,304 on 16 digits, 2,916 Sohei and Butterfly, 3,645 Samurai and Flower** (`structural-max.txt`; the constructed worst grave of each board costs at most 3,321; the Killer and Greater-Than rows of that file, 729 each, are the uncaged / uninequalitied models of the test helper: real cages and signs refuse the technique). The limit is **50,000 per call** with a counter and a parent-budget cadence of its own (checked at the entry and at most every 256 units; `SG.wingSearch.BUG_PLUS_ONE_WORK_LIMIT`, the seam `SG.wingSearch.bugPlusOne(model, state, ctx, { workLimit })`; no adjustment). The structural bound limits the tally operations, **not the total execution time or the initialisation cost** of a call. A spent limit is `null` with `capHit: 'work'`, a spent parent budget is `null` with `capHit: null`, cancellation and every other error propagate. **Records.** `ctx.options.onPatternWork` gets `{ id: 'bugPlusOne', work, limit, capHit, retained: null }` **once per call that returns normally**, including a call whose guard refused at no cost (work 0); none on cancellation, an unexpected error or `assumeUniqueness: false`, and the record is published only after the finder has returned (IR72 C2). The Unique Rectangle shares the bounded-pass helper, now taking the record id as its first argument; its ids, order and deferred publication are unchanged (all 435 earlier wing tests pass). **Observed on the final source** (`work-census.txt`): of **7,168 sampled calls 29 tallied (the guard passed) and 29 found a Finding**, none was capped, the largest work is 288 and the latency of a mean call is 0.0003 to 0.0042 ms (a census in a locked but not protected process, not a promise). Pattern cost (`pattern-cost.json`, 4 cases of 20 runs): work 243 and 351 on the two positives, 0 on the two negatives, none capped, p95 at most 0.115 ms.

**Reachability of BUG+1 (seed 4242).** In 30,000 dug puzzles (original givens, `logs/explore-dug-4242-30000.txt`) 25,294 are solved by the techniques that come before BUG+1; of the other 4,706 positions **62 are a BUG+1 at the point where the registry reaches it** (0.2 % of the puzzles), the guard passes in exactly those 62, all 62 agree with the unique solution, none was capped and the work was 243 to 405. In 20,000 complete solves (`logs/explore-solves-4242-20000.txt`) **98 (0.49 %) use a BUG+1 step**, 43 of them with BUG+1 as the hardest technique. A random state at 9, 12 and 16 digits almost never meets the pattern: the sweep of `techniques` has **no random firing at 4, 9, 12 and 16 digits (3 at 6 digits)** and takes its required minimum of 3 from the three real supplements of `core/tests/fixtures/techniques.js` (reported separately from the random firings).

**Effect of BUG+1 on the references, hashes and goldens (MS11c).** R20 authorizes the reclassification that follows from a correct computation; the historical v2 and external labels are untouched. Of the 26 labelled references **none changes score, level, requiredness, hardest technique or `v3Expected` entry** and none uses a BUG+1 (`rated-effect.txt`, each rated with the registered ladder and with BUG+1 unregistered). Of the 520 regression hashes **3 change: `classic9/nightmare` seeds 377 and 987 and `diagonalX/nightmare` seed 2**, two of the four cells whose level admits the technique; the other 18 keys of `classic9/nightmare`, the 19 of `diagonalX/nightmare`, the 40 keys of `classic9/master` and `jigsaw9/master`, and the 440 keys of the 22 cells below Master are unchanged (**no key below Master changed**). The cause is shown by regenerating the 80 admitting keys with and without BUG+1 in one process (`hash-cause.txt`): the same 3 differ and none is inconsistent, and without it every hash equals the previous record, because the digger rates its candidate puzzles with the registered ladder, a BUG+1 step takes the place of a heavier step in some of them, and so a different candidate is accepted or refused. All five replay wires (G09 to G13) and all 14 analyses (G01 to G14) match the published ones and nothing was written. The pins that follow from the registry are listed with their before and after values in `docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS11c/evidence.md`: the Master proposal of `digger-target` moved from seed 16 to seed 30 (the stuck-proposal coverage of more than 30 fell to 27 at seed 16, where BUG+1 finishes the below-chain solve of seven of nine stuck proposals, 31 total keeps 22 scored + 9 stuck becoming 32 keeps, 30 scored + 2 stuck; the first seed from 1 to 30 with at least nine genuine stuck keeps, excluding the retained seed 7 and the rejected seed 16, is 30 with 15), and the help catalogue holds 36 examples (the 35 earlier ones unchanged on their own sources).

**The XY-chain (rating v3, MS12).** `xyChain` (group `chain`, base cost 82, weight 1000, order 186, between BUG+1 and the Forcing Chain; unscoped, `assumesUniqueness: false`) is the restricted form of the chain search: the same breadth-first search as `forcingChain`, over a graph that an explicit **edge mode** of the shared builder restricts to bivalue-only inferences. **The restricted edges.** A *strong* link ("at least one of the two is true") is only the two candidates of a **bivalue cell**: never the two places of a digit in a unit, however complete the unit is, and never a cell with more candidates. A *weak* link ("at most one of the two is true") is only **the same digit in two distinct cells that see each other** (`model.peers`, partial units included), never two candidates of one cell. The restricted graph does not consult the contents of `ctx.completeUnits` or use unit-conjugate strong links. **The proof.** A search starts only from a *false* literal of a bivalue cell and may end only at a *true* literal of **the same digit in a higher-numbered cell**; the edges alternate strong, weak, ..., strong, so a proof has 3, 5, ..., 11 edges (the registered window is 1 to 12, and an even length cannot occur). If the path runs from "*s* is false" to "*t* is true", then at least one of *s* and *t* is true, so that digit is eliminated from every candidate that sees both cells (the cells of the chain included); the **proof establishes exactly that and no more** (no placement and no contradiction is searched, a start that is "true" has no use, and the two polarities of one node are never compared). A path may visit a cell twice (soundness never depends on the chain being simple), and every edge, both ends and the target set are checked independently by the tests. The scan keeps the **shortest** proof of the whole scan, 1 to 12 edges, with ties to the lowest start node and then the order of the breadth-first search; the caller's `maxChainLength` can only narrow the window and no seam override can widen the registered finder (12 edges, 50,000 units). **Finding.** `{ technique: 'xyChain', cells (the distinct cells of the chain, ascending), placements: [], eliminations (ascending by cell), i18nKey: 'technique.xyChain.step', i18nArgs: { digit, kind: 'endpoints', length (edges), chains: [[ [cell, digit, truth], ... ]] } }`: one chain in traversal order with its polarities (truth 0 for a false literal, 1 for a true one, the start first), `digit` the digit of both ends, and no `kind` suffix in the key; the arrays are the call's own and the state is only read. The text (both apps, EN and VI) says that at least one of the two end cells holds the digit, so every cell that sees both ends loses it. **The shared builder.** The mode is the option `edges` of `SG.chainSearch.forcing` (`'general'`, the default, or `'restricted'`; anything else is `solver.invalidOption`); four filters of one frozen record select the graph (unit-conjugate strong links, same-cell weak links, the same-digit end test and the polarity proofs), the registered `forcingChain`, `forcingChainLong` and `nishio` never read it, and the general graph is unchanged: the seam in general mode and the three registered finders give the **same Findings and the same observer records on 3,682 calls (1,305 with a Finding) as the base commit `5d49130`** (SHA-256 of the results and records `b5458dd3484951418407082b4bee8980f20d78a5dbcee4c131b6468bcf06007e` for both engines; `legacy-diff-tree-final.txt`, `legacy-diff-base-final.txt`), and the tests interleave the two modes and the default on one model. That compatibility is of the finders, not of full solves: registering an earlier technique changes the positions that the chains meet (below). **The registry priority.** "Shortest" applies *within the restricted search*, not across ids: `xyChain` is registered at 186 and acts before `forcingChain` at 190, so a genuine seven-edge XY proof can precede a four-link general proof at 190 on the same position (the fixture "a contradiction chain of 4 links" is one). Nothing relabels an unrestricted proof as an XY-chain: the finder only returns proofs of its own restricted graph, and the tests check that proof independently of the engine (`xyProblems`, a separate oracle over `model.peers` and the candidates). Priors, orders and weights of the other techniques are unchanged. **MS13 handoff.** The four filter flags describe the **atomic** (ungrouped) graph; the grouped builder of MS13 (built in MS13a, described below) stays inactive until its assigned activation, grouping applies to `forcingChain` and `forcingChainLong`, **not** to the XY-chain, a group node needs its own explicit representation and decoder (the arithmetic `cell * digits + digit - 1` decoding is only for atomic nodes), and the generic seam's overrides never widen the registered XY finder's limits. **The lower-end rule is an equivalent mutant, with this argument.** The rule "a proof ends in a higher cell than its start" is a guard of the search, not a restriction of the deduction, and removing it changes neither the Finding nor the charged work. Reverse the path **and complement every truth bit**: plain reversal is not enough. A strong edge is the pair of candidates of one bivalue cell and a weak edge the same digit in two peer cells, both symmetric, so the complemented reverse of a path from "*s* is false" to "*t* is true" is a path from "*t* is false" to "*s* is true" over restricted edges of the same length L, with the same digit, the same two cells and therefore the same target set (it depends only on the ends). Suppose the dropped rule newly permitted a match of length L from a higher start A to a lower end B. The complemented reverse is a lower-start proof from B, whose false literal is an eligible root (it has a strong edge) and is scanned **earlier** (ascending root order). Its search, limited to the best length so far minus one, finds that proof or another of length at most L, **or the best length is already at most L**; the exhaustion of the work limit would have ended the whole call before, with null. After that root the best length is at most L, so the later search from A is limited to a length **below L**: it can neither find nor select the match, and the same holds for every length below the best, so the searches visit exactly the same edges in the same order. The extra branch can only add intersection checks that no counter meters, **so identical latency is not claimed** (only the same Findings and the same charged work); the argument depends on the symmetric edges, endpoint-only targets, eligible roots, ascending root order and the pruning by the best length, and does not transfer to a different graph or search. The guard stays. Mutation analysis of the finder: **43 in-memory mutants of the shared builder, the search, the window, the limit, the Finding and the registration: 42 detected and 1 equivalent** (this one; 420 finite paired comparisons of Findings and work records found no difference and corroborate the argument, they do not replace it).

**Work, records and truncation of the XY-chain (R10, MS12).** One unit is one **visited edge** of the restricted graph (every edge examined, the proof edge included, revisits of an edge from another literal included, and edges to terminal non-bivalue candidates, which are counted but have no strong edge to leave by). The limit is **50,000 per call** (`NEW_FINDER_WORK_LIMIT`, unchanged), with the cadence of the shared search: the parent budget is checked at entry and before every root and so often that no more than 256 units pass between two checks. A spent limit ends the call with `null` and `capHit: 'work'` and **never returns a partial minimum**, a spent parent budget gives `null` with `capHit: null`, and cancellation and every other error propagate. `ctx.options.onPatternWork` gets `{ id: 'xyChain', work, limit: 50000, capHit, retained: null }` **once per call that returns normally** (a call with no bivalue cell included, at work 0), none on cancellation or an error (IR72 C2). The window (12 edges) reaches only 11, since a proof has an odd length, but the expansions of the twelfth level are charged and visited, so the window is not reduced: depth 12 changes the work and the bounded-prefix behaviour (a mutant with a window of 11 is detected by the work enumeration of the tests). **Structural maximum** (`structural-max.txt`): a start costs at most 2n strong and 2n(P) weak expansions (n cells, P the largest peer set) and there are at most 2n starts, so a call costs at most 4n²(P + 1): 8,192 on 4×4, 67,392 on 6×6, 551,124 on every classic 9×9 board, 866,052 on Diagonal, 656,100 on Hyper, 603,612 on Jigsaw 9, 2,405,376 on 12 digits, 10,485,760 on 16 digits and 17,973,252 on the Samurai. **That crude bound exceeds the limit on every board but 4×4**, so it neither proves that the limit cannot be reached nor guarantees completeness within it; it limits tally operations only, not the total time or the initialisation cost. The two constructed worst cases measured with the registered finder cost at most 20,481 units (16 digits, every cell the pair {1, 2}, where a proof is found at once) and, for the twin of a grid (two digits swapped, no deduction), at most 13,750 (Sohei); neither is capped. **Observed on the final source** (`work-census.txt`, `.json`): of **8,768 sampled calls** (the agreement populations of the tests and the first states of the four multi-grid boards) 5,568 searched, **3,912 found a Finding, none was capped, the largest work was 2,078**, and the mean latency of a population was 0.0017 to 0.0674 ms (a census in a locked but not protected process, not a promise). Pattern cost (`pattern-cost.json`, 4 cases of 20 runs): work 1,102 and 752 on the two positives and 1,116 and 263 on the two negatives, none capped, p95 at most 0.286 ms; no wall-time assertion. No finding follows from the absence of observed caps, and the limit was not adjusted.

**Reachability of the XY-chain (seed 4242).** In 30,000 dug puzzles (original givens, `logs/explore-dug-4242-30000-final.txt`) 25,356 are solved by the 27 techniques that come before it; **of the other 4,644 positions 1,413 are an XY-chain at the point where the registry reaches it** (4.7 % of the puzzles; 77 of 7 edges, 952 of 9 and 384 of 11), every Finding agrees with the unique solution, none was capped and the largest work was 4,861. In 20,000 complete solves (`logs/explore-solves-4242-20000-final.txt`) **1,646 (8.23 %) use an XY-chain step** (2,931 steps: 242 of 7 edges, 1,764 of 9 and 925 of 11) and 653 have it as their hardest technique. The `techniques` sweep over the 120 random states of the shared corpus fires **68 times at 9 digits** (`sweep-firings.txt`) and its three real supplements add 4 more (reported apart).

**Effect of the XY-chain on the references, hashes, wires and goldens (MS12).** R20 authorizes the reclassification that follows from a correct computation; the historical v2 and external labels are untouched. Of the 26 labelled references **none changes level, requiredness, hardest technique or `v3Expected` entry** and **3 change score** (`rated-effect.txt`, each rated with the registered ladder and with `xyChain` unregistered): `escargot` 5,506 to 5,499 (one XY-chain use), `constructed-nightmare-1` 2,739 to 2,572 (one use) and `constructed-nightmare-3` 2,586 to 2,653 (three uses); the other 23 are the same. The level that first admits the technique is **Master**: 1.25 × 1000 = 1,250 is above Expert's maximum and below Master's, so the four cells of the 26 whose level admits it are `classic9/master`, `classic9/nightmare`, `diagonalX/nightmare` and `jigsaw9/master`. Of the 520 regression hashes **18 change: `classic9/master` seeds 144 and 377, `classic9/nightmare` seeds 2, 8, 21, 55, 89, 144, 233, 377, 610, 987, 1597, 4181 and 10946, and `diagonalX/nightmare` seeds 2, 1597 and 6765**; the other 62 keys of the four admitting cells (`jigsaw9/master` among them, all 20 unchanged) and the 440 keys of the 22 cells below Master are unchanged (**no key below Master changed**). The cause is shown by regenerating the 80 admitting keys with and without `xyChain` in one process (`logs/hash-cause.txt`): the same 18 differ and none is inconsistent, and without it every hash equals the previous record, because the digger rates its candidate puzzles with the registered ladder, an XY-chain step takes the place of a heavier forcing-chain step in some of them and so a different candidate is accepted or refused. Of the five replay wires **one changes: G13** (Samurai Nightmare 42 generates another puzzle: 8 givens differ, rating score 2,015 to 2,347, 243 to 251 empty cells, a dig log of 333 entries instead of 301; `logs/wire-cause.txt` shows that without the technique every wire equals the saved one, and G09, G11 and G12, whose levels do not admit it, and G10, Killer Nightmare 42, which does, are unchanged). Of the 14 analyses **four change: G03, G04, G13 and G14** (the recorder reports 62, 4, 11 and 19 fields; G05, G06, G07 and G10 admit the technique and are unchanged, and `logs/analysis-cause.txt` shows that without it every golden equals the saved one in every field): G03 (Classic Nightmare 42) is another puzzle (the first jump 44 to 870 is now 45 to 2,393 and a multi-move jump with no candidates recorded by the generator, the final score 2,049 to 2,393); G04 keeps its puzzle and moves two rating positions (1,953 to 1,948 and the move after it 1,118 to 1,123); G13 is the puzzle of its wire (124 to 128 detail rows, final 2,015 to 2,347); G14 keeps its puzzle and its first jump now ends in 1,686 instead of 1,824, its first use is an `xyChain` (it removes the candidate 12 from R16C4, where the first use was a forcing chain that removed it from R13C14) and its other positions follow.
