```
    │                          the ledger helper (tool-records.js) and the golden projection (replay-recording.js),
    │                          the pure helpers of the recovery profile driver (recovery-profile.js)
    │                          and of the mega Nightmare deep tier (mega-contract.js)
    │                          and of the pattern-cost tool (pattern-cost.js),
    │                          and of the qualification and its merge (qualification-records.js),
    │                          and the lazy corpora of the manual suites (manual-corpus.js)
    ├── suites/                The quick set, run by default: *.test.js (37 suites here + the 18 shared ones of
    │                          core/tests/suites, see §15.2)
    └── manual/                The heavy suites, run with --manual or --all: *.test.js (8 suites, see §15.2)
```

### 12.2 Code conventions

| Topic | Convention |
|-------|------------|
| Namespace | Every file is wrapped in an IIFE and attached to `SG.*`. No other global variable may leak. |
| Strict mode | `'use strict';` at the top of every IIFE. |
| Syntax | ES2020 **without modules**. Use `const`/`let`, arrow functions, template literals, optional chaining. |
| Naming | `camelCase` for variables/functions, `PascalCase` for "classes"/typedefs, `UPPER_SNAKE` for constants. |
| Files | `kebab-case.js`. |
| Comments | **English** in `js/` (for tooling compatibility), and only where the code is hard to follow: logic that is not obvious and the contracts of a module (the header of its file, a parameter or return value that is not self-evident). No JSDoc block on every function. |
| `<script>` order | ns → core → variants → word lists → worker-client → ui → app. **`SOURCE_FILES` in `tests/node-runner.js` is the single source of truth**; the same order is repeated in `index.html` (`<script>` tags), `tests.html` (`SOURCE_FILES`) and — for the core, the variants and the word lists only, never the UI or the client — `js/worker/gen.worker.js` (`SCRIPTS`). `ui-contract.test.js` fails when they drift apart. Adding a JavaScript file means adding it to all four. |
| Error handling | Core errors throw `SG.errors.GenerationError` with a `code`, so that i18n can translate them (`error.<code>`). |
| No DOM dependency | `js/core/` and `js/variants/` are **forbidden** from touching the DOM; DOM, `window` and `localStorage` live only in `js/ui/` and `js/worker/worker-client.js`. `purity.test.js` checks this automatically by grepping. Every UI file must also **load in Node without a DOM** (top-level code only defines functions and pushes locale fragments), and exposes its logic as pure helpers so that UI tests need no browser. |
| No colour literals | No colour literal (`#fff`, `rgb(`, `hsl(`, a colour name other than `transparent`/`currentColor`/`none`/`inherit`) outside `css/tokens.css` and `css/tokens-board.css`; everything else uses `var(--sg-…)` tokens. A test checks it. |
| Formatting | 2-space indentation, single quotes, semicolons, lines ≤ 110 characters. |

---

## 13. Internal API Contracts

> This section is the **contract between the agents working in parallel**. Changing any signature here must be recorded as a breaking change, and every caller must be updated.

### 13.1 `SG.rng`

```js
SG.rng.create(seed?: number) → Rng          // no seed: SG.rng.randomSeed(); the seed is normalised to a uint32
Rng = { seed, next():number, int(n):number, pick(arr):*, shuffle(arr):Array, fork(tag:string):Rng }
```

### 13.2 `SG.bits`

```js
SG.bits.popcount(mask: number): number
SG.bits.lowestBitIndex(mask: number): number     // 0-based, -1 if mask == 0
SG.bits.forEach(mask: number, fn: (value:number) => void)   // value is 1-based
SG.bits.toArray(mask: number): number[]
SG.bits.full(digits: number): number             // (1<<digits) - 1
SG.bits.maskOf(values: number[]): number
```

### 13.3 `SG.model`

```js
SG.model.create(spec: ModelSpec): PuzzleModel
SG.model.validate(model, options?): { ok: boolean, errors: string[], warnings: string[] }
SG.model.initState(model): SolveState
SG.model.cloneState(state, into?): SolveState
SG.model.cellLabel(model, cellIndex): string     // 'R4C7' or 'G2-R4C7' for multi-grid
SG.model.isSolution(model, values): boolean
```

### 13.4 `SG.constraints`

```js
SG.constraints.propagate(model, state, budget): boolean    // false = contradiction
SG.constraints.assign(model, state, cell, value): boolean  // peer elimination only
SG.constraints.eliminate(model, state, cell, value): boolean
SG.constraints.place(model, state, cell, value, budget): boolean   // assign + propagate
SG.constraints.registerExtension({ id, prepare?(model), propagate(model, state, data, ctx) }) // cages, inequalities…
```

### 13.5 `SG.dfs`

```js
SG.dfs.solve(model, givens: Int32Array, rng?, budget?): Int32Array | null
SG.dfs.countSolutions(model, givens, limit = 2, budget?): number
SG.dfs.stillUnique(model, givens, cell, trueValue, budget?): boolean
SG.dfs.searchFrom(model, state, { rng, budget, limit, maxBacktracks }): { found, solution, backtracks, aborted }
                                                  // randomised completion of a propagated state; what the full-grid generator restarts (not part of the original contract)
```

### 13.6 `SG.logicalSolver` and `SG.techniques`

```js
SG.logicalSolver.solve(model, givens, options): SolveReport
options = { allowedTechniques: string[]|null, collectSteps: boolean, budget, stopOnStuck: boolean,
            assumeUniqueness: boolean }          // false: skip techniques with assumesUniqueness (uniqueRectangle)
SolveReport = {
  solved: boolean, status: 'solved'|'stuck'|'contradiction', state: SolveState,
  steps: Array<{ technique, i18nKey, i18nArgs, cells:number[], eliminations:Array<[cell,value]>, placements:Array<[cell,value]> }>,
  techniqueCounts: Object<string, number>, hardestTechnique: string, score: number /* uncalibrated, see §6.6 */,
  solution?: Int32Array                          // a DFS solution when stopOnStuck is false and the loop got stuck
}
SG.techniques.register({ id, group, baseCost, order, i18nKey, variantScope, assumesUniqueness, find(model, state, ctx) → Finding|null })
SG.techniques.list() / get(id) / has(id) / appliesTo(tech, variantId) / GROUPS
Finding = { technique, cells, placements:[[cell,value]], eliminations:[[cell,value]], i18nKey, i18nArgs }
// The window kernels of tech-chain.js (§6.6); the five registered chain techniques are wrappers that call them with explicit windows
// and work limits (xyChain 1..12 restricted edges and nishio 1 unbounded on the singleton kernels; nishioDeep 2..3 at 50,000; forcingChain 1..C and
// forcingChainLong C+1..12 call the grouped seam below, with the bounded attempt of 4,096 nodes and 50,000 units shared by the two stages):
SG.chainSearch.forcing(model, state, ctx, { id, minLength, maxLength, workLimit?, edges? }) → Finding|null   // inclusive range of the shortest proof, in edges; edges: 'general' (default) | 'restricted'
SG.chainSearch.groupedForcing(model, state, ctx, { id, minLength?, maxLength?, groupLimit?, workLimit?, fallback? }) → Finding|null   // MS13c: the bounded grouped attempt; fallback true (forcingChain) runs the complete singleton search after a local cap
SG.chainSearch.nishio(model, state, ctx, { id, minDepth, maxDepth, workLimit? }) → Finding|null      // inclusive range of the least depth
// The grouped chain graph (MS13a, §6.6; read by forcingChain and forcingChainLong since MS13c, by nothing else). Nodes are { cells (ascending), digit (1-based) }:
// the singletons, then the groups of a complete box/region x row/col intersection (windows and diagonals make none), sorted by [digit, ...cells]:
SG.chainGraph.build(model, state, ctx, { groupLimit?, workLimit? }?) → { nodes, weak, strong, complete, work, capHit }   // limits 4096 groups / 50000 units (SG.chainGraph.LIMITS); an incomplete graph has empty arrays and capHit 'work' | 'nodes' | null
ctx.options.onPatternWork({ id, work, limit: number|null, capHit: null|'work'|'records'|'nodes', retained: number|null })   // optional observer; forcingChain and forcingChainLong add domain, grouped { construction, search, additional, graph, minimum, stop } and fallback
// The almost-locked-set enumerator (MS15a) and ALS-XZ (MS15b, §6.6; core/js/engine/tech-als.js; the enumerator publishes no observer record, ALS-XZ publishes one for the whole call):
SG.alsPatterns.enumerate(model, state, ctx, work, { cells?, records? }?) → { records: [{ cells, digits, occurrences: { digit: cells } }], complete, capHit: null|'records'|'work' }   // sets of 1..4 open cells of one unit with k+1 digits, canonical order, a prefix when cut; limits SG.alsPatterns.LIMITS = { cells: 4, records: 4096, work: 50000 }
SG.alsPatterns.createWork(limit?) → work = { limit, used, checkedAt, capped }; SG.alsPatterns.spend(work, ctx) → boolean   // the one per-call work guard, shared by the enumeration and the pair scan, never reset; the budget is checked at entry and every 256 units
SG.alsPatterns.findXz(model, state, ctx, { workLimit?, limits? }?) → Finding | null   // the registered `alsXz` finder passes no options (the production LIMITS); the options are the seam of the tests; one onPatternWork record { id: 'alsXz', work, limit, capHit: 'work'|'records'|null, retained } per call that returns
SG.alsPatterns.findSueDeCoq(model, state, ctx, { workLimit?, limits? }?) → Finding | null   // MS16, the registered `sueDeCoq` finder (the same seam and the same record shape with id 'sueDeCoq'); an absent workLimit is its own private SDC_WORK_LIMIT (50000), not LIMITS.work; `opts` must be an object (an array or null is refused by both finders, `solver.invalidOption`); an explicit `limits` is checked even when the geometry ends the call before the enumeration
```

### 13.7 `SG.difficulty`

```js
SG.difficulty.LEVELS: string[]          // ['veryEasy','easy','normal','hard','expert','master','nightmare','hell1','hell2']
SG.difficulty.BASE_GROUPS: string[]     // ['basic','locked','subset','fish','wing','chain'] (names only; no longer defines levels)
SG.difficulty.WEIGHTS, TABLE, SCORING   // weights (§6.6), bands and clue floors (§6.7), formula constants
SG.difficulty.RATING_VERSION            // 2
SG.difficulty.spec(level, model): DifficultySpec
DifficultySpec = { level, min, max, minClues, maxClues, minCluesPerUnit, allowedTechniques:string[],
                   floorTechniques:string[], tierTechniques:string[] /* §6.7 */,
                   allowedGroups:string[], symmetryDefault, extraPasses /* unused */, oracle: 'techniques',
                   proposal: boolean /* nightmare, hell1 and hell2 */, scale }
SG.difficulty.floorTechniques(level, model): string[]           // the floor of a level (§6.7): the ids the previous global level
                                                                // allows, [] for veryEasy; any valid level; a fresh array
SG.difficulty.tierTechniques(level, model): string[]            // the tier: what the level allows beyond its floor; a fresh array
SG.difficulty.certifyFloor(model, givens, level, budget): ReferenceFloorResult   // reference check, below
SG.difficulty.requiredOf(rating, level, model): FloorResult | null   // the same verdict from a canonical rating, below
FloorResult = { required: boolean, vacuous: boolean, floorLevel: string|null }
ReferenceFloorResult = FloorResult + { unsolvedCount: number }  // cells the floor techniques leave unsolved
SG.difficulty.classify(report, model): Rating
Rating = { level, score, rawScore /* = score */, hardestTechnique, hardestGroup, confidence, solved, empties,
           techniqueCounts, ratingVersion }       // no complete proof: level, score and rawScore are null
SG.difficulty.rate(model, givens, options?): Rating            // solve with every technique, then classify
SG.difficulty.isSupported(level, model): boolean                // pre-check before generating
SG.difficulty.weightOf(techniqueId): number                     // throws 'difficulty.unknownTechnique'
SG.difficulty.breakdown(counts, empties), scoreOf(counts, empties) // the formula of §6.6, exact parts / integer
SG.difficulty.scoreTable(counts, empties), apportion(values, total) // the Difficulty score table (§10.5)
SG.difficulty.bandOf(level), levelOfScore(score), levelIndex(level), provesUniqueness(rating)
```

`floorTechniques` and `tierTechniques` throw `difficulty.unknownLevel` for a level that does not exist and do not check that the variant offers it (`spec` does, and still throws `difficulty.unsupportedLevel`); without a model they are unfiltered, like `spec`. `certifyFloor` is a reference for tests, soak runs and other tools, not a production path. It solves with the floor techniques alone (`assumeUniqueness: true`, `collectSteps: false`, `stopOnStuck: true`) and relies on the caller that the givens have exactly one solution for this very model (cages and signs included): it counts no solutions and completes nothing by search. Its only limit is the caller's `budget` (or `null`), checked once at entry and handed to the solver; it has no cap of its own. A solved floor gives `{ required: false, vacuous: false, floorLevel: <previous level>, unsolvedCount: 0 }`; a stuck one gives `required: true` and `unsolvedCount` = the cells left (`report.state.unsolvedCount`); `veryEasy` and a level with an empty tier give `{ required: true, vacuous: true, floorLevel: null, unsolvedCount: 0 }` without a solve (the budget is still checked first). A contradiction is an error, never a certificate: `GenerationError('unexpected')` with `i18nArgs { reason: 'floorContradiction', level }`. Invalid input (`solver.invalidGivens`, `difficulty.unknownLevel`), cancellation and budget failures (`budget.cancelled`, `budget.exceeded`) propagate unchanged and are never read as "stuck".

`requiredOf(rating, level, model)` is pure: no solve, no solution count, no claim detection and nothing persisted. Its cases, in this order: a missing rating or one with `solved !== true` gives `null` (no certificate; the level is not even looked at); `veryEasy` and a level with an empty tier give `{ required: true, vacuous: true, floorLevel: null }`; otherwise `{ required: <some id with techniqueCounts[id] > 0 is not in floorTechniques(level, model)>, vacuous: false, floorLevel: <the previous global level> }`, also for a level or a previous level the variant does not offer. An unknown level throws `difficulty.unknownLevel` once a solved rating is given. It equals `certifyFloor` only under the preconditions that the caller guarantees and that it cannot detect: the floor ids are a prefix of the ladder of the variant (asserted by the tests, §6.6); the rating and the reference belong to the same model, givens, candidate initialisation and finder defaults (a rating of a model whose cages or signs changed is not recognised as stale: rate that model again); uniqueness may be assumed because the caller has proved it; and the rating is one complete canonical result of `SG.difficulty.rate` / `SG.digger.rateCounted`, not a restricted `classify` report or an imported claim. `certifyFloor` stays the independent check, with its own inherited budget only.

### 13.8 `SG.variants`

```js
SG.variants.register(def: VariantDef): void       // validates the definition; throws variant.invalidDef
SG.variants.get(id): VariantDef                   // throws variant.unknown
SG.variants.has(id): boolean
SG.variants.list(): VariantDef[]
SG.variants.optionsOf(idOrDef): NormalizedOption[]
SG.variants.defaultOptions(idOrDef): { [optionId]: default }
SG.variants.sanitizeOptions(idOrDef, raw): { [optionId]: value }   // clamp, snap to step, coerce, drop unknown keys; never throws
VariantDef = { id, i18nKey, group, digits, difficultySupport, defaultSymbolMode,
               symbolModes, options: OptionSchema[], build(opts, rng): PuzzleModel,
               postGenerate?(ctx): PuzzleModel|void   /* + optional twin postGenerate.steps(ctx) generator */,
               digStrategy?: string, fastSeed?(model, state, rng): boolean,
               restartThreshold?: number, clueFloor?: table|function, clueFloorScale?: { level: factor } }
NormalizedOption = { id, type: 'slider'|'select'|'boolean'|'text', labelKey, hintKey, default, min, max, step,
                     minLabelKey, maxLabelKey, defaultLabelKey, choices:[{value,labelKey}], advanced,
                     visibleWhen, maxLength, placeholderKey }
```

The option schemas that the variants declare differ in small ways (`i18nKey` or `labelKey`, `hintKey` or `hintI18nKey`, `choices[].i18nKey` or `.labelKey`); the registry **normalises** them into the one shape above, and the UI (`controls.js`, `store.js`, the cache key) only ever reads `optionsOf` / `defaultOptions` / `sanitizeOptions`, never `def.options`.

### 13.9 `SG.pipeline`

```js
SG.pipeline.run(config: GenerateConfig, emit: (e: GenerationEvent) => void | Emitter | null, budget?): PuzzleResult
SG.pipeline.runSteps(config, emit, budget?, { now, cooperative = true, metricIntervalMs }): Generator<undefined, PuzzleResult>
SG.pipeline.normalizeConfig(config): GenerateConfig        // defaults filled in, seed fixed, everything validated
SG.pipeline.toWire(result): Object                         // PuzzleResult as plain data that survives postMessage
SG.pipeline.fromWire(wire): PuzzleResult                   // rebuilds the model, switches the lazy extensions on
SG.pipeline.STEPS, DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS (8: the reference of the boards of up to 81 cells, not a default for the others), FULL_TRACE_MAX_CELLS (300)
GenerateConfig = { variantId, difficulty = 'normal', seed?, symbolMode, symmetry = 'rotational180', digOrder = 'random',
                   maxAttempts = SG.digger.defaultMaxAttempts(cells of the board): 8 (up to 81), 4 (up to 144) or 2,
                   trace: 'off'|'summary'|'full' (default 'summary'; the page sends none), variantOptions: Object }
SG.digger.defaultMaxAttempts(cellCount): 8 | 4 | 2         // the one size policy; SG.variants.cellCountOf(idOrDef) gives the cells without a model
PuzzleResult  = { model, givens, solution, rating, exact, seed, config, timings, attempts, warnings, digLog, work, target }
```

`run` is `runSteps` driven to its end without ever yielding (`SG.budget.drive`): same events, same puzzle. The `config` of the result is the normalised one (its `trace` is the one that was applied). `exact` is a boolean: the digger's verdict for the returned puzzle (a complete canonical proof whose level is the requested one and that needs a technique of that level's tier, `SG.difficulty.requiredOf`, §6.8), the same value as the `exact` argument of the `done` event. It is never inferred from the level names of the rating and the request. `toWire` always writes it as a strict boolean (a result without the field is written as `false`) and `fromWire` reads `wire.exact === true`, so a wire without the flag — a puzzle cached before it existed — is not exact; the wire version stays 1. `SG.cache` stores it with the rest of the wire, and an older entry shows as not exact until the puzzle is generated again. `digLog` is how the puzzle was dug: the dig log of `SG.digger.generate` for the sample that became the puzzle — plain data whose entries are listed in [§7.3](#73-replay-engine-in-the-ui-layer) and in the header of `js/core/digger.js` (Greater-Than: `digSignsSteps` in `js/variants/greater-than.js`). It does not depend on `config.trace`; `toWire` / `fromWire` carry it (`fromWire` gives `undefined` when the wire has none), `SG.cache` strips it when it stores a puzzle, and the UI builds its replay from it. `work` is internal to the generator and its tools — not part of any exchange format, export or option: the counters of each attempt of the generation, `{ ratings, proposals, maxRatings, maxProposals, localSearches: [{ ratings, proposals, firstShallowAt, shallowRatings, shallowLimit, capHit }] }` (§6.8 says what each field counts), plain numbers, `null`s and the strings `'shallow'`, `'ratings'` and `'proposals'`, and no time. It is the `work` of `SG.digger.generate`, an owned copy: `toWire` writes a fresh deep copy of it (and leaves the key out when the result has none), `fromWire` gives a fresh deep copy again, and so do `SG.cache.serialize` / `deserialize`, so no side shares an array or a record with another. A wire or a cache entry from before the work existed, or with a work that is not of that shape, gives `work === undefined` — unavailable, never an empty list or zero; the wire version stays 1. `target` is internal in the same way (not part of any exchange format, export or option): the target of the sample, search or kept candidate that is the result (`GeneratedPuzzle.target`, §6.8), a finite number (zero included) or `null`: the sample draws it in a finite band, and it is `null` for an open band, for a result that comes from a variant's structure search (which has none) and for a wire or cache entry from before the field. `toWire` always writes it (`null` for a result without the field, and for any value that is not a finite number), `fromWire` and `SG.cache` read it back, and a wire or a cache entry from before it gives `null`, never a midpoint of the band and never a value inferred from the score; the wire version stays 1. Errors are `SG.errors.GenerationError`s with codes such as `variant.unknown`, `difficulty.unsupportedLevel`, `rng.invalidSeed`, `symmetry.unknownKind`, `digger.invalidOption`, `pipeline.invalidOption`, `budget.exceeded`, `budget.cancelled`; each has an `error.<code>` text. A level that cannot be reached is **not** an error: the best puzzle is returned with the warning `difficulty.approximate`, and `difficulty.shallow` as well when it is in the band but needs no technique of the tier.

### 13.10 `SG.workerClient`

```js
SG.workerClient.generate(config, onEvent, hints?): Promise<PuzzleResult>   // hints: { random } lets the cache serve a pre-generated puzzle
SG.workerClient.cancel(): boolean
SG.workerClient.replay(result, { onProgress, allowMain: false }?): Promise<Analysis>   // the replay analysis; main-thread execution requires allowMain: true
SG.workerClient.cancelReplay(): boolean
SG.workerClient.mode: 'worker' | 'main'
SG.workerClient.ready(): Promise<'worker'|'main'>
SG.workerClient.create(options?): client            // independent instance, same interface + dispose()
```

Details — sessions, batching, the cooperative cancel protocol with the 150 ms terminate fallback, the main-thread slices with their granularity limit and the replay job — are in [§9.3](#93-web-worker--fallback-mechanism); the message protocol is the header of `js/worker/gen.worker.js`. `replay` without `allowMain: true` never runs the analysis on the page: it rejects with `budget.cancelled` and `i18nArgs.reason === 'replayConsentRequired'`; in worker mode it needs no consent.

### 13.11 `SG.i18n`

```js
SG.i18n.setLocale(code): string           // returns the current language after the call
SG.i18n.t(key, args?): string
SG.i18n.current: string                   // 'en' | 'vi'
SG.i18n.applyTo(rootEl): number           // how many elements were updated
SG.i18n.onChange(fn): () => void          // returns an unsubscribe function
// Implemented in addition (see §11.1):
SG.i18n.tError(errOrCode, args?), eventText(event, options?), setCellLabeler(fn), cellLabel(cell, labeler?),
SG.i18n.detect(sources), normalize(code), has(key), keys(locale), formatNumber(n, options?), formatDuration(ms),
SG.i18n.mergeFragments(), conflicts, available
```

### 13.12 `SG.boardView`

```js
SG.boardView.create(svgEl, model, opts): BoardView
BoardView = { applyPatch(patch, { animate?, highlightClass? }), setValues(values, states?), setGivens(givens), showSolution(solution),
              highlight(cells, cls?), unhighlight(cells), clearHighlights(),
              setSignMarks({ hidden, removed, kept, cause } | null) /* Greater-Than signs by index; `cause` gets a separate frame rect; survives a rebuild */, setSymbolMode(mode),
              setLayerVisible(name, flag), isLayerVisible(name), setColorAuxSymbol(flag), setAnimate(flag),
              setInteractive(flag), setOnCellClick(fn), setShowCoords(flag), setModel(model) /* rebuilds, keeps values */,
              refreshText(), pulseDone(), getCellElement(i), getGeometry(), getState(), toSvgString(), toPngBlob(scale?), destroy() }
// Pure helpers (run in Node, no DOM): SG.boardView.geometry(model, opts), renderMarkup(model, state, opts), exportSvg(...), cellView(...)
```

### 13.13 `SG.digger`, `SG.budget` and `SG.events` (the stepper API)

```js
SG.digger.generate(model, level, rng, options): GeneratedPuzzle     // the restart loop: grids × dig samples
SG.digger.generateSteps(...)  digLevelSteps(...)  digStageSteps(...)  digSteps(...)   // generator twins that yield at the
                                                                     // natural boundaries when options.cooperative is set
SG.digger.dig / digStage / digLevel                                   // stage A / A+B / one attempt (dig → rate → tune)
SG.digger.registerStrategy(id, fn)  getStrategy(id)  unregisterStrategy(id)   // 'cells' (default), 'killer', 'signs'
SG.digger.createStageSteps(emitter, options)                          // the stage runner the pipeline hands to generate
SG.digger.ORDERS                                                      // ['random','rowByRow','snake','spiral','jumpOne']
SG.digger.targetPolicy  removalObjective  createKeeper  keeperView  keptPuzzle   // internal seams for tests and the activation (§6.8): not a public API
SG.budget.create({ ms, nodes, onCancel, shouldCancel, now })          // check() · tick() · cancel() · elapsedMs()
SG.budget.createClock(rawNow)                                         // active clock: pause()/resume() around each slice
SG.budget.drive(steps) / isSteps(x)                                   // run a stepper to its end
SG.events.createEmitter(verbosity, sink, options) / toEmitter(x)      // + the patch, view and trace helpers of §7.2
```

### 13.14 `SG.app` — the extension API of the UI

`js/ui/app.js` owns the page and is the only file that new UI modules talk to; the full list is the header of the file. In short: `getStore() getBoard() getPlayer() getStepView() getResult()` (→ `{ result, trace }` or null), `generate(overrides?) regenerate() cancel() showResult(result, { trace })`, `setClient(client)`, `attachTrace(trace, digLog?)` and `markTraceUnavailable()` (what the cache uses), `toast(kind, key, args)`, `announce(text)`, `openModal({ titleKey, body, footer, wide, onClose })`, and an event bus `on(name, fn) → off` / `emit(name, payload)` with the events `generate:start generate:progress generate:end generate:error generate:cancel result trace replay:consent position locale theme settings action`. `position` carries `{ position, length, step, direction }` after every move of the replay (`step` is a step of `SG.player.buildViews`, null at position 0; `direction` is `'forward'`, `'backward'` or `'seek'`); `trace` carries `{ result, trace }` once `attachTrace` gave a puzzle served from the cache its replay; `replay:consent` is emitted when the user presses "Prepare score tables" (the cache waits for it in main-thread mode), and `markTraceUnavailable()` tells the preparation that the log of a cached puzzle cannot be had, so the fallback replay is shown. The buttons emit `action { id: 'export' | 'exportCode' | 'play' | 'settings' | 'help' }` (Print, Export and Play at the top right of the main panel, Settings and Help in the header). At the end of start-up `app.js` calls `init(SG.app)` — idempotent, in its own `try/catch` — on each of `SG.exportUi`, `SG.a11y`, `SG.theme`, `SG.help`, `SG.cache`, `SG.history` that exists, in this order; so a new module plugs in **without editing `app.js`**. `SG.cache.init` replaces the generation client through `SG.app.setClient` with a wrapper of the same interface.

### 13.15 `SG.exchange` and the Play-online API of `SG.exportUi`

`js/ui/exchange.js` (no DOM; loaded before `js/ui/i18n.js`; shared with Sudoku Player). The format, the token and the errors are described in [§10.9](#109-hand-off-to-the-player-exchange).

```
SG.exchange.FORMAT / PARAM / ACTIVE_PARAM / MAX_JSON_BYTES / MAX_TOKEN_CHARS
                                                                   // 'sudoku-generator/v1', 'puzzle', 'active', 8 MiB, 4 Mi characters
SG.exchange.encode(doc, { compress? }) -> Promise<string>          // token: 'z.' + deflate-raw, or 'j.' plain
SG.exchange.decode(token) -> Promise<Object>                       // the document (not validated)
SG.exchange.encodeText(text, { compress? }) -> Promise<string>     // text token of a bundle: 't.' + deflate-raw, or 's.' plain
SG.exchange.decodeText(token) -> Promise<string>                   // the text
SG.exchange.isTextToken(token)                                     // 't.' / 's.' (a bundle) as against 'z.' / 'j.' (one document)
SG.exchange.plain(value)                                           // typed arrays -> arrays, undefined/functions dropped
SG.exchange.playerUrl(base, token, { active? }) / isAllowedBase(base)
                                                                   // '<base>#puzzle=<token>[&active=<n>]'; http(s) or relative only
SG.exchange.tokenFromHash(hash) / activeFromHash(hash) / isDocument(data) / canCompress()
SG.exportUi.toJson(result, ctx?)                                   // the §14.1 document, now with `model`
SG.exportUi.buildPlayLink(result, { base?, generatedAt?, locale?, compress? })
                                                                   // -> Promise<{ url, token, base, length, long }>
SG.exportUi.buildPlayBundle(results, { base?, active?, compress? })
                                                                   // -> Promise<{ url, token, base, count, active, length, long }>
SG.exportUi.playerBase(content?) / pagePlayerBase()                // <meta name="sg-player-url"> or the default
SG.exportUi.openPlayLink(url)                                      // window.open(url, '_blank', 'noopener')
SG.exportUi.playOnline() -> Promise                                // every puzzle of the saved list, the one on screen active, opened in a new tab, toasts (the Play button)
SG.exportUi.PLAYER_URL_DEFAULT / LONG_LINK_CHARS                   // '../player/index.html', 100000
```

---

## 14. Data Formats

### 14.1 JSON schema of a puzzle

```json
{
  "format": "sudoku-generator/v1",
  "generatedAt": "2026-10-15T11:00:00.000Z",
  "variantId": "jigsaw9",
  "variantOptions": { "compactness": 0.6 },
  "difficulty": { "requested": "hard", "achieved": "hard", "score": 502,
                  "hardestTechnique": "xWing",
                  "techniqueCounts": { "nakedSingle": 21, "hiddenSingleBox": 14, "nakedPair": 3, "xWing": 1 },
                  "rawScore": 502, "hardestGroup": "fish", "confidence": 0.68, "solved": true, "empties": 54,
                  "ratingVersion": 2 },
  "seed": 1734567890,
  "symmetry": "rotational180",
  "symbolMode": "digits",
  "digits": 9,
  "cellCount": 81,
  "layout": { "rows": 9, "cols": 9,
              "cellRegion": [0,0,0,1,1,1,2,2,2, "..."],
              "subGrids": [{ "gridIndex": 0, "originRow": 0, "originCol": 0 }] },
  "givens":   [0,0,3,0,0,0,0,8,0, "... 81 values, 0 = empty cell"],
  "solution": [5,1,3,7,2,9,4,8,6, "... 81 values"],
  "cages": [],
  "inequalities": [],
  "stats": { "clues": 27, "elapsedMs": 412, "attempts": 1,
             "timings": { "configure": 0.1, "buildModel": 9.3, "fullGrid": 3.2, "extraStructure": 0.1,
                          "dig": 380, "rate": 11, "tune": 0.2 } },
  "symbols": { "mode": "digits", "labels": ["1","2","3","4","5","6","7","8","9"] },
  "digOrder": "random",
  "maxAttempts": 8,
  "warnings": [],
  "locale": "en",
  "labels": { "variant": "Jigsaw Sudoku", "requested": "Hard", "achieved": "Hard", "hardestTechnique": "X-Wing" },
  "model": { "variantId": "jigsaw9", "digits": 9, "cellCount": 81, "units": [[0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8], "..."],
             "unitMeta": [{ "kind": "row", "gridIndex": 0, "label": "R1" }, "..."], "cages": [], "inequalities": [],
             "layout": { "rows": 9, "cols": 9, "cellRow": ["..."], "cellCol": ["..."], "cellRegion": ["..."],
                         "overlays": [], "subGrids": [{ "gridIndex": 0, "originRow": 0, "originCol": 0 }] },
             "symbols": { "mode": "digits", "labels": ["1","2","3","4","5","6","7","8","9"] },
             "fixedCells": [0,0,0, "... one value per cell"] }
}
```

Everything from `symbols` down is **additive**: the first plan's fields are unchanged. The export (`SG.exportUi.toJson`, the document of the player link) writes the following beyond the plan: `rawScore`, `hardestGroup`, `confidence`, `solved`, `empties` and `ratingVersion` inside `difficulty` (rating v2: `ratingVersion` is 2 and `rawScore` equals `score`; the format stays `sudoku-generator/v1`, and a reader that does not know `ratingVersion` ignores it); `stats.timings` (milliseconds per step); `symbols` (the symbol mode and the label of every value, so Mega 16×16 and Wordoku are described exactly); `digOrder`, `maxAttempts` and `warnings` (the configuration and the warnings of the run); `locale` and `labels` (rule L6: the names of the variant, the level and the hardest technique **in the language of the export**); `model` (the plain-data model spec for Sudoku Player, see [§10.9](#109-hand-off-to-the-player-exchange)); and, only where applicable, `layout.cellRow` / `layout.cellCol` (the canvas position of every cell, written for boards that are not a full row-major rectangle — the multi-grid boards, whose canvas has holes) and `wordoku` (`{ word, placement, line }`, Wordoku only). `cages` carry `{ cells, sum, distinct }`; `inequalities` carry `{ a, b }` (`value[a] < value[b]`).

**No import:** the generator no longer reads puzzle files, pasted text or dropped files (the former `SG.importUi` was removed together with the JSON, text, PNG and SVG exports). The document only travels to the player through the link of [§10.9](#109-hand-off-to-the-player-exchange).

**Compatibility rule:** the player checks `format` (`sudoku-generator/vN`) and rejects another version. New fields may only be added in a backward-compatible way.

### 14.2 URL parameters

| Parameter | Example | Meaning |
|-----------|---------|---------|
| `v` | `v=jigsaw9` | Variant ID |
| `d` | `d=hard` | Difficulty |
| `seed` | `seed=1734567890` | Seed (missing ⇒ random). A link (or a reload) with a seed generates that puzzle as soon as the page loads |
| `sym` | `sym=rotational180` | Symmetry type |
| `mode` | `mode=letters` | Symbol set |
| `order` | `order=snake` | Dig sequence: `random` (default), `rowByRow`, `snake`, `spiral`, `jumpOne` |
| `attempts` | `attempts=12` | Maximum full grids to try, 1–20. Written only when the number was chosen (an explicit `attempts=2` on mega16 stays in the link); a link without it takes the default of its own variant: 8 up to 81 cells, 4 up to 144, 2 above |
| `sol` | `sol=1` | Show the solution next to the puzzle |
| `o.<id>` | `o.cageSize=2` | A variant option, e.g. `o.compactness=0.8`, `o.futoshiki=1`, `o.placement=row` (sanitised against the variant by `SG.variants.sanitizeOptions`) |
| `lang` | `lang=vi` | Language |
| `auto` | `auto=1` | Generate automatically as soon as the page loads (a link with a `seed` does so anyway) |

`?v=classic9&d=master&seed=42&lang=vi&auto=1` must always reproduce the **exact** same puzzle.

- Only values that differ from the defaults are written to the address bar. The page has no share-link button any more (the Result panel lost it); `SG.store.shareUrl` / `toSearchString(state, { full: true })` still build a link that always carries `v d sym mode` as well, and the address bar of the page itself is such a link once a seed is in it. Any other parameter is ignored and left out of the canonical URL — the `trace` and `speed` of older links included (the "Replay detail" option and the replay speed are gone, §10.4). The page never starts a generation because the URL changed; it generates **on load** when the URL has a `seed` (a shared link, or a reload after "Generate random", which writes its new seed to the URL) or `auto=1`.
- **Seed hashing.** The state keeps the seed as the text the user typed. A decimal integer in 0…4294967295 is used as it is; anything else (letters, a negative number, a number beyond uint32) is hashed to a uint32 with FNV-1a 32 bit over the UTF-16 code units of the NFC-normalised, trimmed text — the same hash `SG.rng` uses to derive child streams. A blank seed means "random" and the `seed` of the configuration is left out.
- **Priority at start-up:** URL > stored values > defaults; when the URL carries any puzzle parameter (`v d seed sym mode order attempts sol o.*`), the URL is the whole puzzle configuration and the stored configuration is ignored — a shared link must not be bent by the receiver's last settings.
- **Attempts: default or chosen, and replays.** The default number of attempts is the one of the selected variant's board (`SG.store.defaultAttempts`, §6.8, §10.4), so a link without `attempts` for a larger variant takes that variant's number and never the 8 of the page's start-up variant. An `attempts` parameter, and a number typed in the control, is a chosen number whatever its value (`attemptsExplicit` is `true`), also one equal to the default, and a link keeps it (`?v=mega16&attempts=8` runs 8 attempts; `?attempts=8` is never migrated). A puzzle that is generated again from its recipe — a history entry opened, a cached puzzle whose record no longer matches (`app.generate(overrides)`, which sets the store with `{ source: 'app' }`) — runs with the number the recipe recorded, so an older mega16 entry made with 8 still runs 8 (the same engine gives the same puzzle; nothing promises it across rating versions); the export (`toConfig(Object.assign(defaults(), overridesOf(entry)))`) does the same, a recorded number winning over the default marker `defaults()` carries. A replay is not a new choice: a recorded number equal to the replayed variant's default leaves the intent as it was (default stays default, a chosen number stays chosen, now equal to that number), any other recorded number becomes a chosen one, and a replay never turns a chosen number into a default. A change made in the form (`source: 'controls'`), a link or a direct caller of the pipeline is not a replay. A state that says "default" but carries a number other than its board's (the marker `defaults()` hands on in `Object.assign(defaults(), { maxAttempts: 3 })`) is read as a chosen number: the number wins, and nothing is ever read as a default from equal values alone.

### 14.3 `localStorage` keys

| Key | Contents |
|-----|----------|
| `sg.prefs.v1` | `{ lang, theme, fontSize, reducedMotion, colorAuxSymbol }`: language, theme, font size, reduced motion, colour-blind aux symbol (a `v` or a `speed` stored by an older version is ignored and disappears at the next write) |
| `sg.lastConfig.v1` | `{ variantId, difficulty, seed, symmetry, symbolMode, digOrder, maxAttempts, attemptsExplicit, showSolution, variantOptions }`: the most recent generation configuration (a `v` or a `trace` stored by an older version is ignored). `attemptsExplicit` is the intent of `maxAttempts`: `true` when the number was chosen (typed, in an `attempts` parameter, or recorded by a replayed puzzle that is not at its board's default), `false` when it is the default of the stored variant. A record **without** the marker was written before the size defaults: a last configuration saved before size-dependent defaults that holds 8 without an intent marker is read as the default, on every board, because it cannot be distinguished from a deliberately chosen 8; entering 8 again makes it an explicit choice (CV6-04); any other valid number is kept as a chosen one; the next save writes the marker, and until then loading the same record again gives the same state. History recipes, exported puzzles and a link's `attempts` are never migrated. The marker never leaves the store: not in `GenerateConfig`, the history, the cache or an export |
| `sg.cache.v1` | `{ version: 2, entries: [{ key, cfg, at, pool, data }] }`: up to 20 generated puzzles (LRU, `pool: true` marks a pre-generated one; at most 2 per configuration, 4 in all), with version checking (version 2 = rating v2: the puzzles cached by an older version are purged once). `data` is `SG.cache.serialize(result)`: the `toWire` form with typed arrays encoded as `{ $t, d }` plus a checksum of `givens` + `solution`. **No trace and no dig log are stored.** |
| `sg.history.v1` | `{ version: 1, entries: [...] }`: the list of generated puzzles shown in the left panel (newest first, at most 100). An entry is `{ variantId, requested, level, seed, clues, score, symbolMode?, symmetry, digOrder, maxAttempts, variantOptions, ratingVersion? }` — what the list shows and what rebuilds the puzzle (the recipe: the same engine and rating model give the same puzzle for it, nothing promises it across rating versions; no grid is stored). `ratingVersion` (MS17a) is optional and is the `rating.ratingVersion` of the result the entry was made from: a positive integer copied as the result stated it (never the current version filled in for a result or an old entry that has none, never coerced or rounded from a string or a fraction); a missing or malformed one is read as missing and omitted at the next write while the recipe stays, a future positive one is kept, and it is not part of the `id`, of the overrides that rebuild the puzzle, of the cache or of an exported puzzle. The envelope stays `version: 1` (another envelope version reads as an empty list), the entries stored before the marker have none and are not claimed to be of any version, and they keep their stored level and score, which are not re-rated. The `id` of an entry (`SG.history.keyOf`) is derived from the recipe fields when the list is read; the `id`, `at` and `cells` fields that older versions stored are ignored and disappear at the next write |
Every read/write is wrapped in `try/catch` (Safari's private mode can throw): blocked or missing storage means the values live in memory for the session (an in-memory LRU for the cache), a `QuotaExceededError` evicts the least recently used entries and retries, and a corrupt item or another schema version is purged. Nothing throws into the application.

---

## 15. Testing Strategy

### 15.1 Infrastructure

Two ways to run **the same test suites**:

1. **`tests.html`** — open it in a browser (it works from `file://`); it shows green/red results, has a suite filter and a text filter. Query parameters: `?suite=killer&filter=text&bail=1&autorun=0`. It loads the quick suites; `?all=1` also loads the manual ones, `?manual=1` runs only the manual ones, and `?suite=<name>` works for both.
2. **`node tests/node-runner.js`** — runs headless, with exit code ≠ 0 on failure. **This is how the AI agents verify their work.** Supports `--all`, `--manual`, `--suite=a,b` (any suite, quick or manual), `--filter=text` (case-insensitive part of the full name), `--exact="<full name>"` (every test whose full name is exactly that; not with `--filter`), `--list-tests=<file.json>` (register the selected suites as a run does, write the inventory, run no test, hook or benchmark), `--results=<file.json>` (run, and also write the result of every selected test; not with `--list-tests`), `--bail`, `--verbose` and `--help`. The two file options take a new project-relative `.json` path inside the repository (never overwritten, not inside the fingerprinted sources) and are implemented by `../core/tests/node-evidence.js`: the inventory rows are `{ suite, ordinal, fullName, kind, skipped, timeoutMs }` (`ordinal`: the zero-based index within the file suite before any filter, so equal names stay apart and the number does not depend on the selection), with the `sourceHash` and the list of files it covers (the runner, the harness, the loaded and read sources, the fixtures, every discovered quick suite, the HTML source lists, `AGENTS.md`, the guides and this design, the manual suites only when one is selected), the Node version, the time factor, the registration time and the registration errors; the result file holds the run's own rows plus `ordinal`. `complete` is false for a registration error, a bail, a test that did not run or sources that changed during the run; it never says a test passed. It loads `SOURCE_FILES` (a missing file is skipped), then `../core/tests/fixtures/*.js` and `tests/fixtures/*.js`, then discovers `../core/tests/suites/*.test.js` and `tests/suites/*.test.js` (and `tests/manual/*.test.js` with `--manual` or `--all`) by itself; there is no registration step for a new suite (`tests.html` keeps hand-written `SUITE_FILES` and `MANUAL_SUITE_FILES` lists for browser runs, and `ui-contract.test.js` checks that they match the two directories).

**Two sets of suites, so that development stays fast.** The **quick set** is the 37 suites of `tests/suites/` and the 18 shared ones of `../core/tests/suites/` (which the player runs too) — 55 suites, 4,769 tests, what `node tests/node-runner.js` runs by default and what developers and coding agents run while they work. A complete quick set can take longer than one bounded command may (an evidence command is stopped at 540 seconds): then it is covered by disjoint shards (`--suite=<names> --results=<file.json>`, a describe or full-name selector for one long suite, each selection checked with `--list-tests` first), so that every test runs exactly once, with no `--bail`, no omitted slow test and no added skip. Its cost is the sum of the shards, not the limit of one command, and what it measured on a named source and machine is in [docs/difficulty-v3/recovery-quick-cost.md](../docs/difficulty-v3/recovery-quick-cost.md) (a report, not a promise). `tests/manual/` holds the 8 **heavy** suites (`variants`, `killer`, `greater-than`, `perf`, `difficulty`, `extra-regions`, `wordoku`, `jigsaw`; 1,148 tests together: every variant at every level, the performance budgets, the regression hashes, and the four cases that MS07f moved from the quick set: the three generation-cost ratios of the events in `perf` and the two real mega16 replays in `variants`, see §15.2), which are not part of the default run. Run them with `node tests/node-runner.js --manual` (only these) or `--all` (quick and manual: 63 suites), or by name with `--suite=killer`, which works for any suite. The rule of thumb: the quick set after every change, a heavy suite by name when you touch its area, `--all` before a release and whenever you touch generation, scoring or a variant. A heavy suite whose corpus is large is run **one level at a time** (§15.4, "The corpora of the manual suites"). CI runs the quick tier only, never `--all`: the pull-request and deploy workflows (`.github/workflows/pull-request.yml`, `.github/workflows/pages.yml`) run the quick set in the groups of `.github/scripts/generator-quick.json`, which `.github/scripts/generator-suites.sh check` verifies against the registered quick set (every quick suite and test in exactly one shard, no manual or unknown suite, no empty shard, every job inside its 30 minutes, so that a suite added later fails the check until it has a group), and the manual suites, the deep tier of `tests/mega-contract.js`, `soak.js`, `perf.js` and `qualify.js` are replaced there by offline evidence, listed in [docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS05r6c/evidence.md](../docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS05r6c/evidence.md). Since MS07f (owner decision S-OD8) the three generation-cost ratios of the events (the `generating puzzles` tests, in `perf`) and the two real mega16 requests of the replay case of the `player` suite (now the case `(mega16)` of `variants`) are manual, unchanged and still hard failures, and **no pull request runs them**; the structural event tests, the short digging and full-grid cost tests, the classic9 Hell 1 replay case and the golden replays G01-G14 stay quick. The quick set keeps a thin guard over what the heavy suites cover: `tests/suites/smoke.test.js` generates one puzzle per variant and checks its uniqueness, level and determinism and a sample of the regression hashes.

`tests/harness.js` provides: `describe` / `it` (and `.skip`), `expect(v).toBe/toEqual/toBeTruthy/toBeFalsy/toBeNull/toBeUndefined/toBeDefined/toBeGreaterThan[OrEqual]/toBeLessThan[OrEqual]/toBeCloseTo/toBeInstanceOf/toMatch/toContain/toHaveLength/toThrow`, `beforeEach` / `afterEach`, `bench(name, fn)` for performance measurement, `SG.test.run(options)` (`suite`, `filter` or `exact`, `bail`) and its read-only twin `SG.test.list(options)`, which walks the registry the same way and returns the selected rows and the registration errors without calling a test, a hook or a benchmark, and `SG.test.skip(reason)` for environment limits. Tests may be async. Suites that need the file system read `SG.test.node` (`{ fs, path, vm, root }`, Node only). `SG_DEEP=1` (Node) or `SG.test.deep = true` (browser) switches the mega, multigrid and variants suites to their full statistical volumes. The time checks of the suites (CV6: generation time against the §9.1 budgets) use `SG.test.timeLimit(budget)`, the budget times `SG.test.TIME_FACTOR`: 2 by default, 3 when the environment variable `CI` is set, or `SG_TIME_FACTOR` (1: the budgets themselves). They guard against a gross slowdown on any machine, a busy one included; the budgets as such are enforced by `tests/qualify.js` (§6, docs/difficulty-rating/qualification.md) and measured by `tests/perf.js` (§9.1).

### 15.2 Test suites

The 63 suites: the 55 of the quick set (run by default) — the 37 of `tests/suites/` and the 18 of `../core/tests/suites/` (`bits`, `board-view`, `constraints`, `exchange`, `harness`, `logical-solver`, `model`, `solver`, `symbols`, `tech-*`, `techniques`, `wordbook`) — and the 8 of `tests/manual/` (marked **manual** below; `--manual` or `--all`). The current test count is in the developer guide (`../DEVELOPERGUIDE.md`); the one of the combined project is in `docs/qa/final-review-v1.md`. Fixtures live in `tests/fixtures/` and `../core/tests/fixtures/`: `classic.js` (puzzles with known answers), `techniques.js` + `techniques-helper.js` (positive / negative fixtures, the optional `supplements` of the soundness sweep and the soundness helper), `chain-proofs.js` (`SG.chainProofs`, MS13a: the independent checker of the grouped chain graph and of its proofs, §6.6), `jigsaw-models.js` (`SG.fixtures.jigsawModels`, MS14a: the Jigsaw 9 builder, layout check, transpose, restarting solver and six named test-only layouts of the inactive Law of Leftovers kernel's tests, §6.6), `testkit.js` (the boards of the shared suites; `tests/fixtures/testkit-provider.js` plugs in the variant registry), `rated.js` (puzzles labelled with their level, and with `v3Expected`, the level and the required verdict of the current rating), `perf-helper.js` (the budgets of §9.1 and the hash corpus: the 520 `(combination, seed)` records, the selection and command-line plan of the hash modes of `tests/perf.js`, their ledgers and complete merge) and `regression.js` (see below), `difficulty-diagnostics.js` (the pure helpers of the soak's `--json` diagnostics, §15.4), and the data of the replay suite: `replay-goldens.js` (the golden numbers of 14 requests: details sequences, compact rows, jumps) and `replay-wire.js` (the saved results of the five slow ones, G09-G13, generated by `tests/replay-fixtures.js`), with the helpers of their recorders: `tool-records.js` (ledgers, complete merge for records kept as `{ key, request, value }` or as flat fields, field differences and the level guard: pure, reusable by other recorders, and used by `tests/perf.js`) and `replay-recording.js` (the projection of an analysis to a golden record, the canonical text of `replay-goldens.js` and the command line of both recorders), and `recovery-profile.js` (the pure helpers of the recovery profile driver, §15.4) and `mega-contract.js` (the pure helpers of the mega Nightmare deep tier: the fixed population, the checks, the records and their reconciliation, §15.4) and `manual-corpus.js` (`SG.test.lazyCorpus`, the lazy per-level corpora of the manual suites, and the helpers that read a manual suite in memory without running it, §15.4) and `pattern-cost.js` (the pure helpers of the pattern-cost tool: command line, case plan, statistics, record and its completeness check, §15.4) and `qualification-records.js` (the pure helpers of the qualification: the protocol constants and the 15 × 9 manifest, the decision of `tests/qualify.js`, the check of a cell and a shard and the complete merge of the 135 cells, §15.4).

| Suite | What it checks |
|-------|----------------|
| `smoke.test.js` | The quick stand-in of the manual sweeps: the 15 registered variants, one puzzle per variant at its easiest level (unique, `givens ⊆ solution`, the requested level), a level a variant does not offer is refused, eleven cells of the regression table with the first fixed seed (hash unchanged), classic9 `hard` twice with the same seed, Killer and Greater-Than at `normal` |
| `bits.test.js` | The foundation primitives: namespace, `errors`, `popcount` / `lowestBitIndex` / `forEach` over every mask for `digits ≤ 16`, `budget` |
| `harness.test.js` | The test harness itself: `expect`, deep equality, the runner and its isolation |
| `rng.test.js` | Determinism per seed; `fork` independence; unbiased `shuffle` (χ² test) |
| `purity.test.js` | `js/core/` + `js/variants/` contain no `document`/`window`/`localStorage`; there is no `import`/`export` anywhere in `js/`; it also self-tests the harness |
| `model.test.js` | Correct construction; V1–V8; symmetric `peers`; no coordinate collisions; the classic boards of `SG.classicModel` |
| `variants-registry.test.js` | The variant registry (`SG.variants`) and the classic variants; the size of every board (`cellCountOf`, a declared `cellCount`, the 144, 189, 288 and 369 cells of the multi-grids against the models they build) |
| `constraints.test.js` | P1–P3, P6; contradiction detection; soundness against known solutions; the extension hook |
| `solver.test.js` | Solves the sample puzzles correctly; `countSolutions` stops correctly at 2; `stillUnique` **gives results identical to** `countSolutions == 1` on 200 random cases; its speed against the naive check |
| `full-grid.test.js` | Generated grids are valid; same seed ⇒ same grid; uniformity; fixed cells; restarts; `fastSeed` |
| `digger.test.js` | Symmetry groups and stage A: the result always has a unique solution, `givens ⊆ solution`, respects symmetry, `minClues` and `minCluesPerUnit` |
| `digger-target.test.js` | The target policy of difficulty v3 (§6.8), active: the pure helpers, the draw and route of each sample's target, the activated stages and the nullable transport of the target. `targetFor` (the child stream `target` only, the parent streams and other children unmoved, a stream returning 0 and the largest value, no target and no draw without an upper bound, the open band of `SG.difficulty.bandOf('hell2')`), `preferObjective` and `applyBounded` (every row of the tier table, the tier before the distance, ties keep the first, proposals by priority and never against a score, a success never replacing a refused or absent incumbent, a missing target), `preferRated` (the lower quality first, nearer or higher score, exact ties, quality `Infinity`), `acceptReveal` (the reveal vectors of the Expert band with target 850, above-band rule, shallow, under-minimum and non-improving reveals, the floor of the band on its own with target 705, no finite target, a sequence of reveals) and the target of a call at `SG.digger.digLevel` (an explicit number, zero or `null` forwarded as given, an absent one initialized once from the entry's own stream, `null` without a draw for an open band; in `generate` one draw per entered sample from its own child and none for a search). The guided pass (active): 29 real passes, bounded with and without a target and proposal, replayed from their own dig log against an independent table (first-success pools, the apply table, the numeric trace and the oracle calls, including the one evaluation of the pass's own board), and scripted pools (a winner the table refuses ends the pass with no further solve, draw or end record, the all-refused and the empty pool, an unrated or capped own board, cancellation, proposals, cooperative identity); `removalObjective` against the solver's own report (refusal reasons, `tierUsed` and its vacuous cases, the exclusive maximum, the step guard, proposal priorities, ambiguity without a solve). The transport: the keeper views (a scalar copy of every kind of value, a live shared keeper whose prototype is the original keeper, the original receiver, two contexts and suspended generators with different targets, an earlier structure-only winner staying `null`) and the three cases of the sample, the kept candidate and the local search through `generate`, each the sample's own draw computed on a test stream from the known seed and the literal fork path, whatever number, zero, `null`, absent or `NaN` the outer caller passed. The downward refinement (1100 to 920 to 880, with 900, 840 and 690 undone, at or below the target, shallow, no target) and the real `signs` route: Greater-Than Expert, seed 319, one pipeline request through the real strategy, where every invocation gets its sample's own target, the selected result carries the one of the invocation it comes from, and the dig does not read it |
| `logical-solver.test.js` | The technique registry, the solve loop, `variantScope`, `collectSteps` |
| `tech-basic.test.js`, `tech-subset.test.js`, `tech-fish.test.js`, `tech-wing.test.js`, `tech-chain.test.js` | One suite per technique group: published positive fixtures, negative fixtures, and soundness on other boards and seeds. `tech-chain.test.js` also tests the search seam `SG.chainSearch` (§6.6): the range over the shortest proof (a 3-edge minimum rejected by a requested minimum of 5, the minimum of 7 edges on a ring of 13 pair units in [5,12], a shorter proof on a later root, the tie order, chains of more than 12 edges, the longer of the two chains of a proof), the depth range of Nishio, the work limit and its exhaustion (`null`, never the best proof so far), the budget and cancellation, the pattern-work record and the state left untouched, and, since MS07b, the four registered windows on their own (the split at C = 4 and 6, `SHORT_MAX` read at every call, options that only narrow, every proof owned by exactly one id, the explicit work policy of each wrapper against the legacy unbounded one, and 7- and 8-link proofs of real puzzles, whose stuck positions are built with C = 6 (three of the twelve puzzles of that list, #379, #452 and #725, are replaced in place by #3329, #1036 and #2475 of the same seeded stream since MS10b, because the WXYZ-Wing precedes the chains and solves or shortens their stuck positions); the default C is 4 since MS07c, so the 5- and 6-link fixtures belong to `forcingChainLong`), the strict check gate (no more than 256 work units between two checks on stars of 40 to 300 spokes, the exact 256 boundary, an oversized expansion split into pieces, a Finding in the middle of a piece, the unchanged legacy cadence) and a budget that a finder swallowed, which the caller's own check still finds. | `tech-als.test.js` | (MS15a, MS15b and MS16, shared with the player; one suite of the `tech-*` group, apart from the long row above) The bounded almost-locked-set enumerator `SG.alsPatterns.enumerate` (MS15a), the registered ALS-XZ `alsXz` (MS15b, R18) and the registered Sue de Coq `sueDeCoq` (MS16, R19), §6.6. **174 tests**: the 58 of MS15a in eight groups (retargeted where the enumerator is no longer inactive: the scope test now asserts that ALS-XZ and Sue de Coq are the only registered ALS techniques, and the API pin lists `findXz` and `findSueDeCoq`), the 53 of MS15b and the 63 of MS16 (below). The MS15a groups: the scope (the API, the limits 4 cells, 4,096 records and 50,000 units, only `alsXz` registered, the exact shape of the result, no observer record of the enumerator); the vectors (a bivalue cell, two cells {1,2}/{2,3}, a union of 2 or 4 digits that is not a set, three cells with four digits, four with five and the five-cell set that the limit leaves out, cells that share no one unit, solved cells, a set that two units share, an open cell without candidates, a partial cage, diagonal, window and Jigsaw units); the canonical order, also when the units or the cells of a unit are listed backwards; the agreement of every record, the order and the work with an independent enumeration on 84 seeded positions (classic 4, 6 and 9 digits, 12 and 16 digits, diagonal, windows, a partial cage, a Samurai, every named Jigsaw layout and its transpose); the record limit (every lowered limit gives a prefix, exactly the limit is allowed, the probe of the later sizes, the real 4,096 on a 16 x 16 board with two candidates per cell); the shared work guard (a fresh guard, the invalid ones, one unit per tested extension, never reset, the refusal that charges nothing, every limit from 0 to the total giving the prefix of the finished sizes, the default 50,000 cutting a flood); the parent budget (the check at entry and every 256 units at the exact positions, a guard that arrives partly spent, a spent budget, cancellation, other errors, a thrown null, a plain sentinel); and ownership (arrays of the caller's own, read-only inputs under the debug checks, plain data, no cache when the same state object changes, no interference with the registered finders). The independent enumeration is of the records only: it is written from `model.units`, the state and sorted digit lists only (all subsets of each unit's open cells, no pruning; never the engine's masks, search order or pruning). The work and probe counts are NOT independent in that sense: they re-implement the engine's walk (the same order of cells, union bound and size bound) from the definition of a work unit, and so pin the stated count rather than discover it. Inconsistent positions (an open cell without candidates) are structural test inputs, not examples of valid puzzles. A finite check of the size bound (nine allowed cells against eight on a 9-digit board) is part of the vectors. Mutation analysis in `docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS15a/evidence.md`. **MS15b (53 tests in six groups):** the registered technique and the seam (group chain, order 194 between 190 and 198, base cost 106, weight 1,250, unscoped, no uniqueness; production runs with the `LIMITS`, whatever the finder is given); the proof by hand on vectors (the witness of the brief, a single target, overlapping sets, a missing cross-peer, a z cell that the target cannot see, a Killer cage, the pivot cell, a solved cell, no re-check after a cut); the agreement of the first witness and the Finding with an independent oracle (written from the state, `model.peers` and the definitions only) on the real fixture positions and on seeded positions of 4, 6, 9, 12 and 16 digits, diagonal, hyper, cage, Samurai and Jigsaw layouts, every elimination checked against the exact solver `SG.dfs.searchFrom` (forcing the eliminated digit leaves no completion); the one guard, the work limit and the record quota (the units of a pair of two bivalue cells are exactly 22; a cut discards the Finding; `capHit` work before records before null); the parent budget, errors and the observer record (a stop of the parent at any one of its checks gives no Finding and the one record says what the guard had spent; a spent one-shot stop is remembered; an observer that throws is not swallowed); and ownership. Mutation analysis in `docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS15b/evidence.md` (73 finder mutants: 72 detected, 1 equivalent; MS15a's 131 re-run: 130 detected, 1 equivalent). **MS16 (63 tests, 174 in all, in six groups, §6.6 R19):** the registered technique `sueDeCoq` and its seam (group chain, order 196 between 194 and 198, base cost 112, unscoped, no uniqueness, a private 50,000 allowance, the shared seam refusing an array or `null` for both finders, an explicit `limits` checked even on an early geometry negative); the proof by hand (the brief's vector in rows and in columns, both forms of Z, a proper subset of the open intersection as I, solved cells never a target, the near misses: overlapping digits, |C| one too big, one too small, one short for a three-cell I, a digit outside C, an A or B in the wrong unit, and a window, a diagonal or a cage that is neither the line nor the box); the agreement of the first witness, its work and its record with an independent oracle written from `model.units`, `model.unitMeta` and the state on 482 seeded and real positions (rows and columns against boxes and regions, every kind of board, two- and three-cell I, several targets, a first witness that is not the largest, a record-capped call), every elimination checked against the exact solver, and the same Finding when every unit lists its cells from the highest; the one guard (a limit L gives the Finding from the whole count on, every one of the nine charge points, the geometry ending alone, a valid witness without a target, the record quota with its prefix, the work cap before the record quota); the parent budget (the exact checks at entry, every 256 units and at the boundaries, a stop at any of them, a remembered stop, cancellation and other errors, the failed final check) and the observer record; ownership (arrays of its own, read-only inputs under the debug checks, no cache, nothing shared with the enumerator or the other finders). Mutation analysis in `docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS16/evidence.md` (139 Sue de Coq mutants: 131 detected on the first run, 6 equivalent with arguments and 2 killed by tests added afterwards; MS15b's 71 re-run on the final bytes: 70 detected, 1 equivalent; MS15a's 131: 130 detected, 1 equivalent) |
| `tech-cage.test.js` | (MS14c, shared with the player; one suite of the `tech-*` group, apart from the long row above) The two Killer cage interactions that `cageCombo` already implements through `pointOutOfCage` (§6.6, R17: there is no `cageLocked` technique, and the suite pins that the `cage` group is `cageCombo`, `ruleOf45` and `cageSplit`, with `variantScope` `['killer']`): a required digit locked to a unit, and the two-cell Killer pair. 33 tests: 12 positions where the pointing fires (a 1 in every set whose places share a row and a box, only a row, or only a box; a 1 locked again once the only set without it no longer fits the candidates; a placed cell that leaves a pair; a pair of a cage 17 across two boxes, a pair on the diagonal of a box, a pair in a row and a box, a pair settled by typed-in candidates; a set that no open cell can complete; the placed digit that the sets of the open cells must not use) and 4 near misses (a digit missing from one surviving set, a place of the cage outside the units of the others, four pairs of a cage 10, a pair whose cells share no unit), each as given and transposed. The boards are built in the suite with `SG.model.create` from the 27 row, column and box units and one explicit cage, never from a Generator variant (the cage propagation is switched on for the variant id `tech-cage-core` only, and the positions are propagated, so nothing but the pointing is left, except the typed-in and the singles-only ones). Each position is checked against the exact eliminations written out by hand, against a brute-force oracle built from `model.units` and the digit subsets (never from the combination table or the finder's tables), and by the exact solver, which finds no solution with an eliminated digit in its cell; the true solution is kept and the cells outside the common units keep the digit. Mutation analysis in `docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS14c/evidence.md` |
