Six further Node scripts are not part of any suite run:
- **`tests/replay-fixtures.js`** — regenerates `tests/fixtures/replay-wire.js`, the `SG.replay.toPlainWire` data (timings removed) of the five golden requests the `replay` suite cannot generate in a quick run (20 s or more each: Killer Expert 3514204290, Killer Nightmare 42, Greater-Than Expert 42, Greater-Than Hard 3115968914 and, since MS05r6a, Samurai Nightmare 42 (G13), which keeps its request and the recorder's 120,000 ms budget and uses the default 2 attempts of its board; the requests are read from `SG.fixtures.replayGoldens`); with no option it writes the file, `--check` generates them again in memory and compares (exit 1 on a difference). Regenerate only when the generator's output changes on purpose (the regression hashes change then too). It has the bounded modes of the next script.
- **`tests/replay-goldens.js`** — checks and regenerates `tests/fixtures/replay-goldens.js`, the golden data of the 14 requests, from the analysis (`SG.replay.analyse`) of a freshly generated result (the same route as the wire recorder: `SG.store.toConfig`, `SG.pipeline.run`, a 120,000 ms budget), projected by `SG.fixtures.replayRecording.goldenOf` with the comparisons of the golden tests of `replay.test.js` as the field specification (timings and the work counts `checks` are no part of it; `today`, a historical annotation, is kept). With no option it checks, and never writes unasked. Bounded modes, the same for both scripts: `--check` (no writes; exit 1 with the request and the fields that differ), `--write` (the complete fixture; a partial selection is refused), `--ids=G01,G02` (a subset of the manifest; an unknown or duplicate ID is an error), `--record=<file.json>` (the selected requests into a **ledger** `{ schema: 1, kind, sourceHash, manifestHash, node, commit, dirty, records: [{ key, request, value }] }` and nothing else; `sourceHash` is SHA-256 over the engine sources and the recorder code, `manifestHash` over the `{ id, request }` list), `--merge=<directory>` (read the ledgers, require every key of the manifest exactly once from the current sources, then `--check` or `--write` without generating; the records are used in manifest order), `--out=<file.js>` (an alternate project-relative file for `--write`), `--list` and `--help`. Options that conflict are refused before anything is generated, a canonical file is written only after a complete merge has validated (and the old file stays on any failure), and a request whose level qualification has hidden is generated with that level offered for it alone, recorded in the ledger, with the exact support array restored afterwards. `tests/suites/steps.test.js` pins a few step scores by hand: review them together with the goldens.
- **`tests/qualify.js`** — the qualification of the levels each variant offers (protocol: §5.1 and docs/difficulty-rating/qualification.md; options `--variant`, `--level`, `--jobs`, `--json`, `--markdown`, `--probe`, `--no-early-stop`, `--quiet`). A request is a hit only with every independent guard (a valid, unique puzzle with a complete proof, no `difficulty.approximate`, the requested level) **and** a strictly true `exact` flag of the result, so an in-band shallow result is a miss; the predicate is the exported `hitOf`. Since MS07d every shard also records its `qualifierHash`, `acceptance`, `ratingVersion`, the probe and early-stop options, the attempt policy and the size-default `attempts` of each board, and `--merge=<folder> --jobs=4` checks the shards of the 15 × 9 matrix and merges them without generating anything (§15.4).
- **`tests/soak.js`** — see §15.4.
- **`tests/difficulty-recovery.js`** — the recovery profile driver of difficulty v3, a diagnostic of one fixed request that is not a production setting and not a passing performance gate; see §15.4.
- **`tests/mega-contract.js`** — the mega Nightmare deep tier of difficulty v3 and its level-selected Hell 1 population: `--list [--level=hell1]`, `--request=<id> --record=<new.json>` (one of 14 fixed requests of a level, once, with its checks) and `--reconcile=<directory> --json=<new.json> [--level=hell1]` (no generation); a tool, not a suite and not CI; see §15.4.
- **`tests/pattern-cost.js`** — the cost of the technique finders on their real fixtures (difficulty v3, MS07a): `--ids=<id>[,<id>] --runs=20 --json=<new.json> [--case=<text>]` and `--list`; no puzzle is generated; a tool, not a suite and not CI; see §15.4.
- **`tests/perf.js`** — measures every cell of §9.1 (p50 / p95 / max against the p95 target) and maintains the regression-hash fixture: `--update-hashes` records the hashes of the current code on purpose (after a change meant to alter puzzles: a calibration, a new dig rule), `--check-hashes` compares, `--alloc` probes the hot loops for allocation (rule R2), `--gate` fails when fewer than 90 % of the cells meet their target. Both hash modes process the 26 combinations × 20 seeds = **520 records** at once, and the measurement options `--variant` and `--level` do not shard them. For a bounded refresh the hash modes have their own selectors, hash modes only: `--hash-key=variant/level` (one exact combination of `SG.fixtures.perfTools.COMBOS`), `--hash-from=N` (zero-based index in `SEEDS`, 0–19, default 0) and `--hash-count=N` (consecutive seed indexes), `--hash-list` (the selected keys `variant/level#seed` and the identity of the sources, no generation), `--hash-record=<file.json>` (generate the selected records and write **only** that ledger), `--hash-merge=<directory>` (read the ledgers, require all 520 records exactly once from the current sources, then `--check-hashes`, the default, or `--update-hashes`, without generating) and `--hash-out=<file.js>` (an alternate file for a complete update). A `--check-hashes` with a selector says how many of the 520 it checked and is no pass of the complete corpus; a canonical `--update-hashes` needs the complete corpus or a complete merge and is written in CRLF through a temporary file, so any refusal (an unknown key, a range outside 0–19, conflicting modes, a partial update, a missing, duplicate, malformed or stale record) leaves `tests/fixtures/regression.js` byte for byte as it was. The ledger is `{ schema: 1, kind: 'regression-hashes', sourceHash, manifestHash, node, commit, dirty, records: [{ key, variant, level, seed, hash, ms, overrides }] }`: `manifestHash` is SHA-256 over the keys and requests of `COMBOS` × `SEEDS` (never the expected hashes) and `sourceHash` over the engine files the script loads, `perf-helper.js`, `tool-records.js` and `perf.js`. A combination whose level the qualification has hidden keeps its place in the corpus: for that one generation the helper offers the level and restores the exact original support array afterwards (recorded in `overrides`), a diagnostic of the test tools that offers nothing to a player. The command lines are in the developer guide.

### 15.3 Invariants that must always hold

These are assertions that **must never be false**, checked in every generation test:

| # | Invariant |
|---|-----------|
| I1 | `countSolutions(model, givens, 2) === 1` — every generated puzzle has exactly 1 solution |
| I2 | `givens` is a subset of `solution` (it never contradicts the solution) |
| I3 | `solution` satisfies every unit, cage, and inequality |
| I4 | The same `(variantId, difficulty, seed, options)` ⇒ identical `givens`, on the same source. The target policy (§6.8) changes which puzzle a seed gives, by design: the activation refreshed the 520 regression hashes, and the changes are recorded and explained there and in the v3 evidence, not promised away |
| I5 | Every returned puzzle has a complete logical proof (`rating.solved`), and if it is `exact` then `rating.level === requested`, `score` lies within `spec[min,max)` and the proof needs a technique of the level's tier (`SG.difficulty.requiredOf` on the puzzle's own model; nothing is required of `veryEasy`). A complete proof in the band that does not need the tier is shallow: not exact, with `difficulty.approximate` and `difficulty.shallow`. (Rating v2: the level is derived from the score; there is no technique floor.) The soak also requires the `exact` flag of the generator to agree, both ways, with the certificate derived from the canonical rating it returned (a complete rating in the requested level whose counts need the tier) for every puzzle with exactly one solution; a disagreement is an I5 violation, not another kind of miss |
| I6 | Clue count ≥ `SG.difficulty.spec(level, model).minClues` — **the variant's own floor** (§6.7), not the classic9 number |
| I7 | Every logical technique eliminates only values that do **not** belong to the true solution (soundness) |

With `--json` (§15.4) the soak also records every request, a failed one as an error row; a request error, a check that throws or a work conservation mismatch (§15.4) is a failure of the diagnostics next to the violations of I1–I7, even where `--no-gate` silences the exit status. The ledger marks the acceptance it was made under (`meta.acceptance: 'canonical-required-tier'`: requiredness and hits come from the derived certificate of the returned canonical rating) and the short-chain cutoff C it was made with (`meta.chainShortMax`, and `meta.chainWindow`, the `--chain-window` that asked for it, `null` when none did); it is a diagnostic, not an acceptance of the v3 production guarantees.

### 15.4 Fuzz / soak testing

`tests/soak.js` — generates puzzles for every (variant × level) combination, checks I1–I7 on each one, and prints a confusion matrix per variant (requested level in the rows, achieved level in the columns), the time per puzzle against the §9.1 budget, and a score histogram on the bands of the nine levels. Presets: `--quick` (20 per classic9 level and 5 per other offered combination, ≈ 700 puzzles, the P2 checkpoint), `--count=N` (default 500), `--per-combo=N` and `--full` (25 per offered combination, 100 per classic9 level, ≈ 3 550 puzzles, the final QA preset); `--variant`, `--level`, `--seed`, and `--weights` / `--bands` to try other technique weights or bands without editing the file. Exit code 0 requires no invariant violation, ≥ 90 % exact hits on every classic9 level and ≥ 80 % on every other combination (`--no-gate` always exits 0). An **exact hit** is the generator's own `exact` flag, taken strictly: `p.exact === true` where the soak calls `SG.digger.generate` and `r.exact === true` where it runs `SG.pipeline.run` (Killer, Greater-Than, Wordoku, Jigsaw; a missing flag is a miss), never a comparison of the requested and the achieved level, so an in-band shallow result is not a hit on either route; I5 checks every exact puzzle against its score band and `requiredOf`. **The allowance, the cutoff and the shards.** `--shallow-cap=64|128|256|none` sets `SG.digger.SHALLOW_SEARCH_RATINGS` (§6.8) for the run after the sources are loaded (`none` is `Infinity`; absent keeps the default of the source, 256 since task MS05r4, §6.8; any other value is a usage error). `--chain-window=4|6` (difficulty v3, MS07c; a diagnostic, never a production or user setting) sets the short-chain cutoff C of §6.6, `SG.chainSearch.SHORT_MAX`, once, right after the sources are loaded and before the ordered-prefix check, the sampled references and every request, and puts the previous value back when the run ends, whatever the end; any other value (`5`, `0`, `12`, `4.0`, `04`, an empty text ...) is a usage error with exit code 2 before anything is generated, also with `--no-gate`, and so is a source that has no cutoff to set; absent keeps the cutoff of the source (4 since task MS07c, §6.6). The text says `Short-chain cutoff C: …` when the flag is given. `--from=<k>` with `--count=<n>` and `--total-count=<N>` runs the requests k to k + n − 1 of one logical cell of N requests, for exactly one selected variant and level (integers k ≥ 0, n > 0, N ≥ k + n; k > 0 needs `--total-count`; refused with `--quick`, `--full`, `--per-combo` and with more than one cell, exit code 2); the tags stay `variant:level:absoluteIndex` and the direct `base.fork(tag)` and pipeline `seedFor(tag)` routes are those of the whole cell, so the shards of a plan give the puzzles of the unsharded cell. The R5 selection of the default (§6.8, task MS05r4) is computed from such ledgers by `tests/artifacts/difficulty-v3/MS05r4/select-cap.cjs` (the shards of a cell merged by `mergeShards`, the hits from `generationExact`, the upper median of `fullRatings`; a gap, a duplicate, mixed sources or unknown work blocks it). **Ordered prefix.** The derived requiredness (§6.6) holds only while, for every variant, every level and the no-model case, the floor and the floor with the tier are ordered prefixes of the ladder of that variant (the registry order of its weighted techniques, never re-sorted by weight). After `--weights` and `--bands` have patched the tables, and before it generates or draws anything, the soak checks that (`prefixViolationOf` in `tests/fixtures/difficulty-diagnostics.js`, which is loaded with or without `--json`); on a violation it prints the variant, the level, whether the floor or the floor with the tier broke, and both technique lists, and exits 2, also with `--no-gate`. `--weights={"nishio":1}`, which would put a late technique into the cheap tiers, is such a vector; malformed JSON in the option is a different usage error with the usage text. It is not part of any suite run (too slow); it was the tool of the calibrations of tasks `T-2.10` and `T-7.7`. Which levels a variant offers at all is decided by the qualification, `tests/qualify.js` (protocol of report §6; results in [docs/difficulty-rating/qualification.md](../docs/difficulty-rating/qualification.md)).

**Baseline diagnostics, `--json=<file>`** (a path inside the repository; difficulty v3, [report R23](../docs/difficulty-v3/report-detailed.md), measured in [docs/difficulty-v3/baseline.md](../docs/difficulty-v3/baseline.md)). The option adds, without changing how any puzzle is generated (same direct-or-pipeline route, tags, seeds and timing boundary) or what the text above prints, a ledger of every request (schema 1: the puzzle's identity or its error, `scoreHit` = solved in the band of the request, `generationExact` = the generator's own strict `exact` flag (the required tier since MS05b1; the level name alone before it), `qualifiedHit` = solved in band **and** the certificate derived from the returned canonical rating (`SG.difficulty.requiredOf` on the returned model) says the tier is required, `required` and `shallow` the same derived verdict (`null` for a request with no returned puzzle, a puzzle shown to have a second solution or an incomplete rating), the raw and the attainable position of the score with their inputs, the calls of `SG.difficulty.rate` during the generation, the work of the generation (below), the lengths of the forcing chains (`forcingChain` and `forcingChainLong` alike) and the depths of the Nishio steps (`nishio` and `nishioDeep` alike) of the final path, and `target`, the target of the selected result (a finite number, zero included, or `null`, which a result without one has; the soak reads it from either route and never draws or computes one)) and a diagnostic summary after the text. The positions are `raw = (score − min) / (max − min)` and `adjusted = (score − lower) / (max − lower)` with `lower = max(min, 1.25 · wMin + V)`, `wMin` the lightest weight of the level's tier and `V` the empty-cell part of the score; they are neither rounded nor clamped, `lower` is an analytic bound and not a proof that the search reaches it, and `adjusted` is `null` with a reason (`unrated`, `non-finite-band`, `non-positive-width`, `off-band`, `empty-tier`, `vacuous-tier`) where it is undefined. The shallow share is over the solved in-band puzzles that have a derived verdict. **The work.** Every row says what the generation did (MS05r3): `fullRatings` (the sum of the full ratings of the work of its attempts), `ratingCalls` (an independent count of the calls of `SG.difficulty.rate` during the generation only, not the checks after it), `proposals`, `localRatings`, `shallowRatings`, `capHits` (`{ shallow, ratings, proposals }`: the local searches that ended on each) and `work` itself (§6.8 and §13.9), also for a request that failed in the digger (its error carries the work so far); `workCheck` is `{ status, reason }` with `ok` only when `fullRatings === ratingCalls` and the local ratings of every attempt are at most its ratings, `mismatch` otherwise (a rating made outside the counted entry point; it fails the run like a request error) and `unavailable` when the generation reported no work: missing work is never zero and never a passed check, it leaves the diagnostics incomplete, and it blocks any selection that reads work. The new solves — the sampled independent reference floor of the requested level (`SG.difficulty.certifyFloor`, only after I1 proved uniqueness, and only for the first and the last request of the logical cell, indexes 0 and N − 1, once when N is 1; the others say `referenceStatus: 'not-selected'`) and the final path rated again with `collectSteps` (every unique, solved request) — run only after every generation and every check of I1–I7 of the invocation has finished, one budget of 120,000 ms per request, because the subset finder keeps a check-spacing counter across solves (while that counter exists). The reference is kept apart from the derived verdict: `referenceRequired`, `referenceStatus` (`not-run`, `not-selected`, `ok`, `vacuous`, `error`, `skipped-not-unique`) and `referenceError` change neither `required`, `shallow` nor a hit, and a failed reference is its own status, never "required" and never "shallow". The summary counts the unselected references apart (`references.notSelected`), so ten unselected rows are not ten unmeasured verdicts, adds `exactDisagreements` (the producer flag `generationExact` against the derived certificate) and `work` (the counts of the three work checks, the local searches ended by each cap and the distribution of `fullRatings`), and `meta` says `acceptance: 'canonical-required-tier'`, `shallowCap` and `shallowSearchRatings` (`null`: `Infinity`), `chainWindow` (the `--chain-window` of the run, `null` when none was given) and `chainShortMax` (the short-chain cutoff C in force, `null` for a source that has none), `sourceHash` and `toolHash` (the soak and its two helpers), and for the manifest `logicalRequests`, `shard` (`{ from, count, total }` or `null`) and, per cell, `from`, `logicalTotal` and `referenceIndexes`. `mergeShards(ledgers)` of the helper checks that the ledgers of the shards of one logical cell are complete, current and compatible (same sources, tool, Node, seed, allowance, short-chain cutoff, settings and logical total, the span aside: shards of C = 4 and of C = 6, or one that asked for a window and one that did not, are never merged), that every row is inside its span with its tag, and that together they hold the indexes 0 to N − 1 exactly once; a gap, a duplicate or a mixed setting is refused, and the rows of a plan are merged, never the medians of its shards. The ledger file is rewritten after every request and every diagnostic and says `complete: true` only at the end. The pure helpers are `SG.fixtures.difficultyDiagnostics` (`tests/fixtures/difficulty-diagnostics.js`).

**Recovery profile, `tests/difficulty-recovery.js`** (difficulty v3, task MS05r1). A diagnostic command: not a production setting, not an acceptance run and not a passing performance gate. `node tests/difficulty-recovery.js --mode=profile --case=G13|mega12-nightmare|mega16-nightmare --json=<file>` generates one of three fixed requests once and records where its time went, in the ordinary dig samples or in the local continuation that follows them and that a shallow-search allowance can limit. The requests are the actual G13 golden request of `SG.fixtures.replayGoldens` through `SG.store.toConfig` (samurai, Nightmare, seed 42) and the existing seed 3 Nightmare requests of mega12 and mega16 (trace off); the profile changes one setting, `maxAttempts: 1`, read back from the normalised configuration, and keeps `digTries`, the rating and proposal caps, the clue floors, the symmetry and the sample schedule. `--mode=diagnostic --shallow-cap=64|128|256|none` keeps the normal attempts of the request and applies the allowance: the command sets `SG.digger.SHALLOW_SEARCH_RATINGS` for the run (`none` is `Infinity`) and puts the previous value back, and a source without the setting is refused before anything is generated. One process runs one case, and an existing evidence file is never overwritten. The engine and the validation of the returned puzzle (the givens belong to the solution, `SG.model.isSolution`, an independent uniqueness count, and the complete canonical rating of the returned model, from which the requiredness is derived) share one budget of 500,000 ms, below the 540-second limit of a bounded command; a failed validation is not a fallback.

The observation is transparent. For the length of the run the tool wraps the exported `SG.digger.generateSteps` (it hands the digger a stage delegate that times each original stage call in a `try` / `finally` and leaves the body untouched, with `isStepStage`, the same `timings` and `current` of the original, and it copies the `work` of the returned puzzle, which the pipeline drops), `SG.digger.getStrategy` (it hands out a wrapper of the built-in `cells` strategy that copies the counters of the attempt's `caps`; the registry never replaces `cells`, so the wrapper cannot be registered) and `SG.difficulty.rate` (an independent count of the full ratings). Each wrapper delegates with the arguments it got and returns what it got, draws no random number and decides nothing, so the puzzle, its rating, its dig log and the random streams are those of an unobserved run; all three are restored in a `finally`, also after an error. A stage is classified by its arguments, not by a strategy label (the local continuation carries the ordinary label): within an attempt a dig stage with the next sample number starts an ordinary sample, the one extra dig stage with the same last sample number starts the local continuation, and the rate and tune stages after it belong to the same triplet; the tune with `finalize: true`, `fullGrid` and `extraStructure` are the rest. Anything that does not fit (a second repeated sample, a sample number that skips or goes back, a stage outside its triplet, overlapping stages, work that does not reconcile with the returned `work` and the rate count) is an instrumentation problem and the outcome `instrumentation-error`, never an invented split.

The record (schema 1, UTF-8, CRLF) holds the request, the normalised configuration and the effective `maxAttempts`, the source and tool hashes, the commit and whether the tree was dirty, the outcome (`ok`, `generation-error`, `validation-error` or `instrumentation-error`) with its error code, the work of every attempt (`ratings`, `proposals` and their caps, and since MS05r3 the `localSearches` records of §6.8, copied whole from the returned work or from the snapshot of the attempt's caps; the counters of a failed run are the last snapshots and labelled partial, an unobserved attempt is `null`, never zero), every stage with its time and its full ratings, and a summary of the time and the full ratings of the ordinary samples, the local continuation and the rest, with the residual between the observed stage times and the pipeline's own timings. A budget error is an outcome row, with its code and its partial work. Exit code 0 means a record was written (read its `outcome`), 1 that no trustworthy complete record could be made, 2 an invalid command line, path or source. The pure helpers are `SG.fixtures.recoveryProfile` (`tests/fixtures/recovery-profile.js`); the profiles taken with them are reported in [docs/difficulty-v3/recovery-profile.md](../docs/difficulty-v3/recovery-profile.md).

**Mega Nightmare deep tier, `tests/mega-contract.js`** (difficulty v3, task MS05r5m; CV6-03, S-OD3 (a)). A full-attempt Nightmare request is seconds on mega12 (4 attempts by default) and about a minute or more on mega16 (2 attempts), and it ends in a truthful shallow fallback (`difficulty.approximate` and `difficulty.shallow`, `exact` false) more often than not, so the old quick callbacks, with their 6 s and 20 s time assertions, are replaced by contracts that fit a quick run and the full requests move to a tool. The tier is not product qualification, not a discovered `*.test.js` suite, not CI and not the `SG_DEEP` branch of `mega.test.js` (a different 20-seed protocol, not run for it). The quick `mega` suite keeps (1) a real one-attempt mega12 Nightmare request, seed 3 (`generate(…, { maxAttempts: 1 })`), with every invariant of the old test, the requiredness and warning contract and a bounded smoke limit of 15 s before the time factor (30 s here, 45 s on CI) against the unchanged 60,000 ms request budget (measured 5.20, 5.19 and 5.21 s in three runs, so about 5.8 times of margin here), and (2) a scripted mega16 control on the real pipeline and digger (the seams of MS05r5p: a registered dig strategy that returns the whole solution with a shallow rating, with no allowance to spend; at the Expert tier, because the Nightmare tier rates its proposal samples for real) of the size-default stop after two attempts, the truthful two-warning shallow fallback, an explicit number of attempts winning over the default, and a cancelled or exhausted budget that propagates its error in the second attempt, with a shallow candidate in hand, instead of returning the best puzzle so far.

The frozen deep population is, per board, the seven requests of the old tests: the invariant seeds 1 to 4 and the three independent calls of the CV5 test, seed 3 twice and seed 4 (`mega12-invariant-1` … `mega16-cv5-other`, fourteen in all), each Nightmare, trace off, with the default symbol set, symmetry and dig order and no explicit `maxAttempts` (the board default, 4 and 2). Equal configurations keep separate ids: the CV5 seed-3 calls are independent generations. The relations of the old test are kept per board: the two seed-3 calls agree on the givens, the solution and the score, and seed 4 has another solution than seed 3 (plus one extra, not original, comparison of invariant seed 3 with them). `--request=<id> --record=<new.json>` runs one request once in its own process, with its checks, inside one 500,000 ms budget (below the 540 s limit of a bounded command), through the shared timing lock; a killed process leaves no record. The record (schema 1, UTF-8, CRLF; the file is named `<id>.json` and never overwritten) holds the request, the normalised configuration, the effective attempts, the allowance, the identities (SHA-256 of the engine sources, of the two tool files, the commit, Node, machine, the command line), the generation and the check time apart, the outcome (`ok`, `generation-error` or `check-error`; a `budget.exceeded` or `budget.cancelled` error keeps its code and arguments and no puzzle is invented), every check as `true`, `false` or `null` (not evaluated), the digests and the plain wire (`SG.replay.toPlainWire`) of the puzzle. A level that qualification has hidden is offered for the run through `SG.fixtures.toolRecords.withRequestedLevel`, the original support array is restored in a `finally` and the override is recorded. The checks do not trust the digger: the givens belong to the solution, a valid solution with the geometry's own box check, the clue floor, the band and group of the returned rating, exactly one solution, every logical placement and elimination against the solution, the plain wire round trip, the attempts (the board default, all of them when the puzzle is not exact), and the shared contract derived from an independent complete canonical rating: `exact` is true exactly when that rating lies in the requested level and `SG.difficulty.requiredOf` says its tier is needed, `difficulty.approximate` is present exactly when the puzzle is not exact, `difficulty.shallow` exactly when it is not exact and the rating lies in the requested level, and no other warning. A fallback can be valid, but it never waives a check and is never an exact hit. The exit code is 0 when every check passed, 3 when the request ended in an error or the checks could not finish (an availability outcome), 1 for a failed check, 2 for an invalid command line, path or source.

`--reconcile=<directory> --json=<new.json>` generates nothing. Each of the fourteen occurrences must be there exactly once, in a file named after it, with the manifest's request, the default attempts and one identity (engine sources, tool, Node, allowance, budget) across the records; the wire must decode, its digests, its seed, configuration, attempts, exact flag, warnings and rating must agree with the record, and the flags and the pure checks are derived again from the recorded rating and the wire, so a record that claims a wrong exact flag or warning, a tampered wire or digest, a record copied under another id or a stray file is an evidence problem. The document keeps three verdicts apart: generation (which requests returned a puzzle, the error codes of those that did not), correctness (`pass`, `fail` or `incomplete`: every check of every record and every relation) and timing (seconds and margins; a request over the 120 s of the application or the 30 s of the qualification budget is a finding, not a failure here), with a complete inventory never called a passing correctness. A relation that needs the puzzle of a request that ended in an error is `unevaluated`. For each board it reports a guard computed only from a completed fixed population (1.5 times the largest generation and check time, rounded up to 5 s, never above the budget) with its margin, and no guard when a request is missing (a measurement is never extrapolated to an unmeasured seed). Exit code 0: all fourteen reconcile and pass; 3: the evidence is sound but incomplete; 1: an evidence problem, a failed check or a failed relation.

The pure helpers are `SG.fixtures.megaContract` (`tests/fixtures/mega-contract.js`), tested by the `mega-contract` suite on synthetic records made from a small real board and scripted producers. Its `reuse(records, { variantId, level, seed }, { sourceHash, toolHash }, { sha256 })` (the tool's `reuse(directory, query)` adds the identities and the directory) is a read-only consumer of the invariant occurrences of the manifest of the query's level: only they answer (mega16 seeds 1 and 2, mega12 seeds 1 to 3), never a CV5 repeat; the record must match the engine sources, the tool, the normalised configuration, the allowance and the digests now, and an unavailable request is returned as such and never replaced by another puzzle. The CV3 manual corpus does not use it: a runner-loaded suite cannot compute the hashes it compares, so the two mega Nightmare cells of CV3 run live (below).

**Mega Hell 1 (difficulty v3, task MS07b; S-OD4 / CV6-03 (1)).** Once `nishioDeep` made the Hell 1 tier real, the full-attempt Hell 1 requests of mega12 and mega16 moved out of the quick tier by the same rule, with the same population (the invariant seeds 1 to 4 and the CV5 calls of seed 3, 3 and 4 per board, fourteen occurrences, board-default attempts 4 and 2): a second, **level-selected** manifest of the same tool with level-qualified ids (`mega12-hell1-invariant-1` … `mega16-hell1-cv5-other`), its own manifest hash and record kind (`mega-hell1-record`, `mega-hell1-reconciliation`) and relations of the same shape. `--list [--level=hell1]` and `--reconcile=<directory> --json=<new.json> [--level=hell1]` select the level (the default is Nightmare, whose ids, manifest and hash did not change); `--request=<id>` finds the manifest by its id. A level-selected reconciliation is that level's fourteen requests only, never both levels at once or the complete population. The quick `mega` suite keeps the real one-attempt mega12 request of the level (seed 3, a bounded smoke limit of 18 s before the time factor, 36 s here and 54 s on CI, measured 7.74, 7.75 and 7.74 s in three locked runs) and the scripted mega16 control, which runs at the Expert tier and proves the size-default policy for both levels, since the proposal samples of the top tiers are rated for real. The fourteen Hell 1 records are in [docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS07b/mega-hell1](../docs/difficulty-v3/evidence/MS07b/mega-hell1).

**Pattern cost, `tests/pattern-cost.js`** (difficulty v3, task MS07a; the GP-cost evidence of the technique tasks). `node tests/pattern-cost.js --ids=<id>[,<id>] --runs=20 --json=<new.json> [--case=<text>]` measures the registered finders on their real fixtures and `--ids=<id> --list` prints the cases; it is a tool, not a discovered `*.test.js` suite, not CI and not a time assertion. It loads what `tests/node-runner.js` loads, in the same order (the harness, the ordered `SOURCE_FILES`, then `FIXTURES_DIRS`, read from that file so that they cannot drift apart) and no suite, and it generates no puzzle. A case is one positive or negative fixture of one registered technique id: of an unscoped one from the **active** map `byId` of `../core/tests/fixtures/techniques.js` (never the frozen `historyV2`; the key is `<id>:<positive|negative>:<n>`), built with `SG.techHelper.makeState` or `stateAfter` as the technique suite builds it, and since MS14b of a technique scoped to exactly `jigsaw9` from `SG.fixtures.jigsawTechniques` (`../core/tests/fixtures/jigsaw-techniques.js`), built on the region map of the fixture through `SG.fixtures.jigsawModels` and `stateAfter`, never on the classic9 board. Each case runs `--runs` times (default 20, at most 200) the same read-only find with a fresh context whose `ctx.options.onPatternWork` collects the work records of the finder (§6.6; a finder that reports none has none, never zero), and the state is compared after every run. The record (schema 1, UTF-8, CRLF, a new project-relative `.json` outside the fingerprinted sources, never overwritten, one line per case) holds the command line, the SHA-256 of the engine sources, of the loaded fixture files and of the two tool files, the commit and whether the tree was dirty, Node, the machine, the configuration (ids, case text, runs, the 480,000 ms caller budget, the model `classic9`, `jigsaw9` or `mixed`, the planned case keys) and, per case, the fixture (a scoped case also keeps its `model` and `cellRegion`), the outcome (`found` or `none`) and whether it is the one the fixture records, the Finding with its digest, the work records, the cap hit (`null` or `work`), `deterministic` and min / p50 / p95 / max / mean of the time in milliseconds (nearest rank) with the samples. Everything but the clock readings is deterministic: every run of a case must give the same Finding and the same work records, or the record says so (`complete` false, exit code 1). The outcome is also checked against the fixture (MS07b, IR55): a case whose Finding is not the one the fixture records, or a negative fixture that finds something, stays in the record with its measurements but makes it incomplete (a problem keyed to the case) and the tool exits with 1, even when every run agrees and even when the record asserts `complete: true`; a matching case passes, and a case whose fixture records no expectation stays unknown, not a mismatch. The caller budget (eight minutes, inside the 540 s of a bounded command, so run it through `Invoke-Dv3Node`) is one `SG.budget` shared by every find; a finder may turn a spent budget into "nothing found", so the budget is checked again after every find and the clock before every run, and a spent budget ends the run with the cases measured so far and a problem, never with a short sample presented as complete. An unknown id, a scoped technique without scoped fixtures for exactly its scope (or with fixtures of another board), an id without fixtures and a `--case` that selects nothing are refused before any run (exit code 2, like an invalid command line or path); 0 is a complete record, 1 an incomplete or inconsistent one. The pure helpers (the command line, the plan, the nearest-rank statistics, the record, its completeness check, the text) are `SG.fixtures.patternCost` (`tests/fixtures/pattern-cost.js`), tested by the `pattern-cost-tools` suite on fake observations and clocks, on the real fixtures of `forcingChain` and `nishio` with a fake clock, and on the tool as a real process, whose test output goes to a folder of its own for each invocation (`.scratch-pattern-cost-tools/<pid-time-random>/` in the repository root, ignored by git; only that invocation's folder is ever removed); no quick test asserts a wall-clock time. The active fixtures cover the four chain ids since MS07b (real positions, among them 7- and 8-link and depth-2 proofs) and `lawOfLeftovers` since MS14b (seven cases: four positives and three negatives on real region maps; 20 runs, none capped, Findings at 278 to 1,901 work units and the negatives at 3,436 to 5,401 of the 50,000).

**Qualification, `tests/qualify.js`, and its merge** (difficulty v3, task MS07d; R24). A shard is the `--json` of one run (one level, or any bounded set of levels). Besides the machine, the engine and the times it now records `qualifierHash` (the SHA-256 of the bytes of `tests/qualify.js` and of `tests/fixtures/qualification-records.js`), `acceptance` (`canonical-required-tier`: the hit above), `ratingVersion` (what the engine reports: the development version 2 until MS17b, so no checkpoint is a released v3 result), `probe` and `earlyStop` (the protocol options of the run), `guardMs` (120,000), `attemptPolicy` (the rule `SG.digger.defaultMaxAttempts`, `explicitMaxAttempts: false` and the SHA-256 of `js/engine/digger.js`, `js/engine/pipeline.js` and `js/variants/registry.js`, the files that decide the attempts of a board) and, in every level, `attempts` (the maximum of attempts configured for its board, the size default: 8 up to 81 cells, 4 up to 144, 2 above; not the attempts a request used, which a shard does not record). No request names `maxAttempts`, and neither the worker nor the merger inserts an eight-attempt override. The decision (seeds, budgets, percentile, the hit, the verdict, the table) is `SG.fixtures.qualificationRecords` (`tests/fixtures/qualification-records.js`), loaded by `tests/qualify.js` in a context of its own, so that the scheduler and the merge cannot disagree; the scheduler itself (seeds, thresholds, the 40 then 60 requests, the early stop, the worker count and the failure of a run on a lost worker or channel) is unchanged. Every output of the tool is UTF-8, CRLF, with a final line end, one line per field, shard and request, and is written whole to `<file>.partial` and then put in place, all or none: the bytes of an existing output are kept first, and if a later output cannot be put in place the ones already replaced are restored (or removed, when they did not exist), the `.partial` files are removed and the run fails with a message that says so, so that a failure leaves every file as it was and no half file.

`node tests/qualify.js --merge=<folder> --jobs=4 [--json=<file>] [--markdown=<file>]` generates nothing. It reads every `*.json` of the folder (a subfolder is not entered; every such file has to be a valid shard) and writes the record of the 15 variants × 9 levels = 135 cells of checklist Q only after everything is valid; a rejected merge prints its problems on stderr, exits with 1 and writes nothing, so an output that was there stays byte for byte. `--jobs=4` is required (the qualification always runs with four workers), `--variant`, `--level`, `--probe` and `--no-early-stop` cannot be combined with `--merge` (exit 2), and no output is a default: an output inside the folder of the shards, one named twice and the live table `docs/difficulty-rating/qualification.md` are refused. A shard is refused for: no or another `qualifierHash`, source hash, `ratingVersion`, attempt policy, protocol, seed base, acceptance or guard (the current tree decides; a shard written before MS07d has none of the new fields); a probe; jobs other than 4; another machine or Node than the other shards (the commit and the dirty flag are provenance, kept per shard and never compared: the hashes are the identity; the record lists the commits in `engine.commits`), another early-stop setting, or a run that overlaps another in time; a cell twice, outside the matrix or missing; and, in a cell, a request with another key, an index outside 0..99 or twice, a seed other than the FNV-1a of `variant/level/index` on the base 20260601, a time that is negative or not finite, an error with a hit, no outcome, or a hit that its own outcome (valid, solved, no approximate warning, `exact`, the achieved level) does not give, a board size, budget (2 s, 10 s, 30 s) or `attempts` other than the board's, and a summary that is not the one the requests give (recomputed with the same `decide`). A level needs exactly 40 outcomes (the indexes 0..39) or 100 for a verdict, so 30 to 35 hits of 40 without the second round are no verdict; any second-round outcome needs all 40 first-round outcomes with 30 to 35 hits. A level is `stopped` only for `misses` (more than 10) or `time` (more than 5 requests over the budget) and only by the early stop: the proof is the scheduler's `certain`, and since both counts grow by at most one per result a stopped level shows exactly 11 misses (or 6 slow requests) at the stop plus at most `jobs - 1` results that were in flight, a first-round stop shows the indexes 0..n-1, and a second-round stop need not (that round is dispatched from 99 down). An error or a timeout is a recorded miss with its time; a failed run (a lost worker or channel) publishes no shard, so no merge can turn it into a verdict. The record keeps each shard's own metadata (`shards`: file, start, end, seconds, engine, cells), `shardSeconds` (the shards' wall times added up) apart from `wallSpanSeconds` (first start to last end), the `matrix`, the `totals` (requests, hits, errors, `invalid`, offered, hidden, stopped) and the `support` lists derived from the verdicts, with every request as the worker wrote it (fields it does not know are kept); an invalid or non-unique puzzle in any request is a correctness failure, printed on stderr, never hidden. The first checkpoint (MS07e) and the final qualification (MS18c) run one level per command, `node tests/qualify.js --variant=<id> --level=<level> --jobs=4 --json=<folder>/<id>-<level>.json --markdown=<folder>/<id>-<level>.md`, with no `--probe`, then the merge of that folder. In an invocation of one level the second round of 60 requests runs on the one worker that finished the first round, because the others left when its queue was empty: it costs wall time, not a verdict, and the scheduler is not changed here.

**The corpora of the manual suites, level by level** (difficulty v3, task MS05b3; FS22, FS25, FS26). The puzzles a manual suite tests are generated through the real pipeline, once, on first use, and a Killer, Jigsaw, Greater-Than, extra-regions or Wordoku corpus is a whole variant: a filtered I5 test used to build every level of its variant first (the whole Killer corpus alone can take longer than the 540 seconds of a bounded command). Now each corpus is `SG.test.lazyCorpus(keys, build)` (`tests/fixtures/manual-corpus.js`): an object with one enumerable getter per key in the original order, where the first successful read of a key calls `build(key, originalIndex)` once and keeps its array, every later read returns that array, listing the keys or registering a test builds nothing and an error of `build` propagates unchanged without publishing anything. The seed of a request comes from the original index of its key (`700 + 100 · li + i` for Jigsaw, `100 · (li + 1) + i` for Greater-Than and Wordoku, `10000 · (vi + 1) + 100 · li + i` for extra-regions, `1 … n` per level for Killer and the variants sweep), never from the order of the reads, so the requests, their seeds, counts, options and the 240,000 ms request budget of the sweep are those of the whole build, and the aggregate helpers (`corpus()` of Killer, `allPuzzles()`, `ctx.corpus()` of the sweep, which generates every missing level before it returns, so that the test that changes the clue floor after filling the corpus keeps its meaning) still ask for the whole population. The per-level tests of Jigsaw, Greater-Than, extra-regions and Wordoku were already one describe per level; the Killer I5 test, once for the whole corpus, is now registered per level (`> <level>: <count> puzzles > I5:`, a local hit count and the same strict `hits > count · 0.8`), and the CV3 test of the sweep per supported level (`> variants: <id> > CV3 <level>: puzzles at the supported level`, the 2 or 3 seeds of its volume, every per-puzzle check before the aggregate "none of N puzzles hit it" failure) and once per variant for the refusals (`> variants: <id> > CV3 unsupported: other levels are refused`, which builds no corpus); the `CHECKS` entry `CV3` and `sweepVariant(def, { only: ['CV3'] })` are the aggregate of the same two helpers.

The harness lower-cases the full name and looks for the filter as a **substring**, so a selector starts at a describe boundary with `> `: `--filter="> easy: 20 puzzles > I5:"`, with the `> `, picks Easy and not Very Easy (`easy: 20 puzzles > I5:` picks both). The selectors are `> <level>: <count> puzzles > I5:` (Killer, Jigsaw, Greater-Than, Wordoku; `… > CV6:` for the last three), `> <id> <level>: <count> puzzles > I5:` (extra-regions), `> variants: <id> > CV3 <level>:` and `> variants: <id> > CV3 unsupported:`; each selects exactly one test, which the `manual-corpus` suite checks on the real registrations. A cell is run with `--suite=<name> --filter="<selector>" --verbose --results=<file.json>` after `--list-tests=<file.json>` has shown a selected total of 1; A whole-variant filter such as `--filter=I5:` is not a bounded command. With `SG_CORPUS_TIMES=1` (Node) a level that has just been built prints the milliseconds of each of its requests, so a transcript of one cell shows the time of every request. The two mega Nightmare cells of CV3 are generated live, one command each (projected at about 221 s for mega16 and 71 s for mega12 from the records of the deep tier); a cell whose requests all end in a shallow fallback passes every per-puzzle check and fails only the aggregate "none of N puzzles hit it" assertion, an availability outcome, not a correctness failure. `manual-corpus.test.js` reads each manual suite in memory (a registry of its own, `describe` and `it` as no-ops, the pipeline scripted; Node only) and checks the seeded plans, the isolation of a level, the aggregate and the names of the selectors without generating a puzzle of those corpora.

### 15.5 Mandatory manual testing (checklist)

The first plan's checklist: 3 browsers × (desktop + mobile) × (light + dark) × (VI + EN) × (opened via a server + opened via `file://`) · a trial print to PDF · a keyboard-only walkthrough · a screen reader · animations turned off.

**What was actually done:** the page was loaded and screenshotted in headless **Edge** (the only browser installed on the development machine; `docs/` holds three of those screenshots), and the DOM-free parts of every UI module are covered by the Node suites above. **Not done:** Firefox, Safari, a real mobile device, a real screen reader (NVDA / VoiceOver) and a print to a physical printer. The remaining items are recorded in `docs/qa/final-review-v1.md` and as known limitations in the user guide (`../USERGUIDE.md`; the technical details in `../DEVELOPERGUIDE.md`) and §18.

---

## 16. Risks & Mitigations

| # | Risk | Severity | Mitigation |
|---|------|----------|------------|
| R1 | Generating `samurai`/`mega16` is too slow and the browser hangs | High | Worker + budget + Cancel button + the empty board while it runs + automatic `trace` downgrade (no percentage bar: the length of a run is unknown). Outcome: p95 is well inside the budgets (§8.4, §9.1) |
| R2 | Hitting the exact difficulty is harder than expected | High | Technique-bounded oracle (§6.8 ④) + a calibration soak test + always keep an "approximate" fallback with a warning |
| R3 | A logical technique is implemented incorrectly ⇒ wrongly eliminates a candidate ⇒ the puzzle has **no solution** | Very high | Invariant I7 checks soundness on every fixture; `digger` always re-verifies with `dfs` before returning the final result |
| R4 | Opening via `file://` does not work | Medium | No ES modules / `fetch`; cooperative main-thread fallback (§9.3); the developer guide explains how to run a server |
| R5 | A generated Jigsaw layout has no solution | Medium | Validation in the build step + reseeding |
| R6 | Killer cages admit multiple solutions | Medium | The 3-step repair strategy (§8.2 step 5), bounded by search nodes |
| R7 | The two language packs drift apart in their keys | Medium | `i18n.test.js` blocks it immediately |
| R8 | The trace log overflows memory | Medium | `maxTraceEvents` + down-sampling + clearing the old trace; the replay reads the small dig log, not the trace |
| R9 | Animations make the app feel slow | Low | ADR-002: the generator runs at full speed, the finished puzzle is shown at once and the replay is stepped by hand (no playback, no speeds) |
| R10 | Scope creep into building a player | Low | Resolved by the split: the player is its own repository (sudoku-player); this one only hands a puzzle over (task `T-7.8`, §10.9) |
| R11 | The score formula does not match players' perception | Medium | `T-2.10` / `T-7.7` calibrated rating v1 with labelled sample puzzles and soak runs; rating v2 (docs/difficulty-rating/report-detailed.md) uses one formula for every variant whose weights are plain data in `SG.difficulty.WEIGHTS`, engineering priors that a calibration with human solvers can replace (`ruleOf45` and `cageSplit` first) |
| R12 | Several agents editing the same file ⇒ conflicts | Medium | Every task in `task.md` states the **files it owns**; the API contract in §13 is invariant |

---

## 17. Implementation Roadmap

The roadmap below is the first plan; **all eight phases are done** (statuses per task: `task.md` Appendix A; completion summary: Appendix E). The one task that left this repository is `T-7.5`, the trial-play mode, which is now the sudoku-player project; `T-7.8` (the hand-off to it) was added in its place.

| Phase | Name | Goal | Deliverable | Status |
|-------|------|------|-------------|--------|
| **P0** | Foundations | Namespace, RNG, bits, budget, test harness | `node tests/node-runner.js` runs green | ✅ |
| **P1** | Classic core | Model, propagation, DFS, full grid generation, basic hole digging | Can generate `classic9` with a unique solution via Node | ✅ |
| **P2** | Logical solver | Technique ladder, scoring, difficulty oracle | The 6 difficulty levels work, hitting ≥ 90% | ✅ (99–100 % on classic9, §15.4) |
| **P3** | Pipeline & events | 7 steps, event schema, delta patches | Can generate through the pipeline with a full trace | ✅ |
| **P4** | UI shell | index.html, i18n, SVG renderer, step description, replay engine | A usable website for `classic9`, bilingual | ✅ — executed **after** P5 |
| **P5** | Variants (in parallel) | The 12 remaining variants (the 3 `classic*` variants were finished in P1) | Each variant passes `variants.test.js` | ✅ — `T-5.1…5.8` executed **before** P4; `T-5.9` (symbol sets) and `T-5.10` (variants sweep) after P4 had started |
| **P6** | Worker & performance | Worker, fallback, cancellation, hitting the performance targets | Meets the §9.1 table | ✅ |
| **P7** | Polish | Export/print, accessibility, themes, cache, documentation | Passes the §15.5 checklist | ✅ (checklist partly: §15.5) |

**Critical path (as planned):** P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P4. P5 could run as multiple parallel agents right after P3. P6 and P7 could start partially after P4.

**As executed:** P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → **P5 (variants)** → P4 (UI) → P6 → P7 → final QA (`T-7.7`). The variants were built before the UI to avoid merge conflicts on the "hot files" (`locale.*.js`, `index.html`, `node-runner.js`): they declared their strings in `SG.localeFragments`, which became the project-wide convention (§11.1). Work was done by parallel agents, each owning an explicit file list, in waves: 5 technique agents (`T-2.2…2.6`), then 4 + 3 variant agents, then the UI agents, then the worker / cache / polish agents, then final QA and documentation. Every agent read a shared **contract** (rules, API, file ownership) first and reported with the template of `task.md` Appendix C.

For the detailed breakdown of each task, see [`task.md`](./task.md).

---

## 18. Implementation notes & deviations

Everything above has been brought in line with the implementation. This table collects, in one place, where the implementation differs from the first plan or decides something the plan left open, and why. (Decisions D1–D6 are the open points the implementing agents raised; their resolution is in the rows marked D*n*.)

| Topic | What was built | Why |
|-------|----------------|-----|
| Pipeline steps 5–7 (§7.1) | `dig`, `rate` and `tune` are three steps. A missed dig sample repeats dig → rate → tune **on the same grid**, up to 12 samples per grid (30 for symmetries with groups of more than 2 cells); then `retry.difficulty` and a new grid. Events: `dig.start` (args `sample`, `maxSamples`), `tune.hit` / `tune.shallow` / `tune.miss`, `retry.difficulty` | The guided dig shares its rating objective with the rater, and a full grid is much more expensive than another dig of it. The attempt counters ("Attempt n of m") are read from these events; the page no longer shows them while a run goes on (it shows the empty board), but `SG.app`'s `generate:progress` event still carries them |
| Stage B of the dig (§6.8) | A **guided** dig: it rates up to 32 candidate removals per step and applies the one that suits the sample's target score best (tier first, then the nearest score, the highest where the level has no upper bound), never past the band; every level (`GUIDED_FROM_LEVEL` is 0), starting at ≈ 47 % of the cells as clues. `tune.restore` / "dig deeper with a relaxed oracle" were not built | A finished stage-A puzzle is minimal, so a relaxed oracle finds nothing to remove; random minimal puzzles need only singles 3 times in 4, so the hard levels would be hit too rarely |
| Scoring and levels (§6.6, §6.7; rating v2) | One formula for every variant, `round(80·E/(E+40) + P + 0.25·P·log₂(1 + W/P))` from the empty cells and the technique counts only, with the weights of `SG.difficulty.WEIGHTS`; nine levels on fixed bands 0 / 45 / 180 / 400 / 700 / 1000 / 1500 / 2400 / 3600 (`expert`, `hell1`, `hell2` are new; `expert`, `master`, `nightmare` are the levels first named `veryHard`, `expert` and `evil` — see the note after the table of §6.7); no technique floor and no band scaling; a puzzle without a complete proof is unrated and never returned; each variant offers the levels it passes in the qualification. Rating v1 (`SG.difficulty.COSTS`, first-use ×1.5, 0.6 per empty cell, bands scaled by `cellCount / 81`, the technique floor) is gone | The v1 technique floor derived the score from the level, and the scaled bands rated the same technique differently on different boards; the joint report (docs/difficulty-rating/report-detailed.md) compares the candidate formulas on the corpora of both researchers |
| Clue floors (§6.7; **D1**) | `spec(level, model).minClues` is the one floor; a variant declares `clueFloorScale` and / or `clueFloor`; Killer: `{ veryEasy: 40 }` only; invariant I6, the digger, the pipeline, `soak.js` and the variants sweep all use it | The classic9 floor scaled by `cellCount / 81` made hard/master unreachable for Killer and left normal/hard hit by 1–20 % of the digs on the hybrid and multi-grid boards (task `T-7.7`) |
| Levels offered (**D2**, **D3**; rating v2) | Rating v1: `flower` offered `normal` (it had none); `classic6` did not (it had it). Rating v2: every variant offers the levels it passes in the qualification (§5.1, docs/difficulty-rating/qualification.md) | v1: with the per-variant floor `flower normal` was hit by 49 % of the digs (was 9 %); `classic6 normal` stayed at 0–3 %. v2: one protocol decides for every variant |
| `restartThreshold` (**D4**) | `SG.fullGrid.generate` reads the variant's `restartThreshold` unless the caller passes one (Mega: 300 / 400) | It was declared but never wired |
| Option schema (**D5**) | Variants keep their own shapes; the registry **normalises** them (`optionsOf`, `defaultOptions`, `sanitizeOptions`); the UI, the store and the cache key read only those | Parallel agents wrote slightly different schemas; normalising in one place was cheaper than renaming |
| Calibration breadth (**D6**) | Rating v1: costs were tuned on classic9; the other variants calibrate through `clueFloorScale`. `jellyfish` is untestable on real data (never on the solving path of 3 000 natural classic9 puzzles), so its cost only follows the fish factor. `extraPasses` is unused | Classic9 has the highest hit rate; the other boards were already at 95–100 % exact hits and needed speed, not new costs |
| Technique list (§6.6) | 40 registered: 34 classic + 1 Jigsaw (`lawOfLeftovers`, group `locked`, scoped to `jigsaw9`) + 3 `cage` + 2 `ineq`; Hidden Single split by unit kind, Quad split Naked / Hidden, the chain techniques split into a short and a long window (`forcingChain` / `forcingChainLong`, `nishio` / `nishioDeep`), the skyscraper, the two-string kite and the remote pairs between X-Wing and Simple Colouring, the empty rectangle and the finned X-Wing between Simple Colouring and Swordfish, the finned Swordfish between Swordfish and Jellyfish, the WXYZ-Wing between XYZ-Wing and W-Wing, BUG+1 between the Unique Rectangle and the Forcing Chain, the restricted XY-chain between BUG+1 and the Forcing Chain, the ALS-XZ between the Forcing Chain and the long Forcing Chain (order 194), Sue de Coq right after the ALS-XZ (order 196); `order` values in §6.6; **singles are techniques** — the logical solver never calls `propagate` | So that singles are attributed and scored, and `allowedTechniques` means what it says |
| Event contract (§7.2) | `SG.events.VOCABULARY` (`type`, `stepId`, `phase`, `key`, `level`, `args`, `patch`) is the contract; variants append their own entries; `lookup`, `vocabularyKeys`, `eventProblems` check events | One list that the core, the UI, the locale packs and the tests share |
| Stepper API (§9.3, §13.9, §13.13) | `SG.pipeline.runSteps` and the `SG.digger.*Steps` generators yield at natural boundaries; `run` is `runSteps` driven by `SG.budget.drive`; `SG.budget.createClock` is an *active* clock | One algorithm for both modes, so events, puzzle and budget accounting cannot differ, and `cancel` works in a worker (which otherwise cannot read its queue) |
| Worker client (§9.3, §13.10) | `generate / cancel / mode / ready / create / dispose`; session ids; ≈ 30 ms event batches; cooperative cancel, `terminate()` after 150 ms; `MessageChannel` yields; a main-thread mode that runs the same slices; **no `SharedArrayBuffer`**; and, for the replay, `replay / cancelReplay` (the analysis job: one job at a time, run on the page only with `allowMain: true`) | A shared flag needs cross-origin-isolation headers that a plain static server does not send; `requestIdleCallback`/`setTimeout` are clamped or throttled in background tabs. The analysis rates a board after every move of the dig, which is long enough to belong in the worker, and a page without a Worker must not freeze before the user has agreed |
| Lazy extensions (§6.3) | Cage and inequality propagation register on first use (`SG.comboTable.enablePropagation`, `SG.ineq.ensureExtension`); `fromWire` and `SG.cache` switch them on for models they rebuild | The engine's own tests assume an empty extension registry, and a plain page load should not touch the engine |
| Default trace (§3.3, §14.2) | Pipeline default `summary`, which is also what the page gets: it sends no `trace` since the "Replay detail" option was removed; `full` (direct callers, tests) is downgraded to `summary` above 300 cells (Samurai, 369 cells, only) | The replay no longer needs a `full` trace (it reads the dig log); `summary` still feeds the `generate:progress` event of the app |
| Replay (§3.3, §7.3, §7.5, §10.3) | Built by `SG.player.buildViews` from the analysis of `SG.replay` (§7.5, which reads `PuzzleResult.digLog`, the final sample only): a compact view of a few steps chosen so that the score never goes down and a details view of every complete move ("Show all moves"), a score table on every step, explained score jumps, technical work hidden. The score tables are the ratings of the analysis, prepared after the puzzle is shown: at once in a worker; on the page only after the user presses "Prepare score tables". The finished puzzle is shown at once and the replay is moved by hand (first / previous / next / last, slider), with static frames instead of animations. The first version — a player of the trace with Play/Pause, four speeds (`instant` / `fast` / `step` / `manual`), the `speed` preference and URL parameter, the "Replay detail" option (`trace` URL parameter), a steps panel of the seven pipeline steps with a technique-frequency bar chart (`#steps-chart`) and then steps built straight from the dig log, whose table rated the puzzle of a step on the page — was removed | Replaying every event was long and mostly technical (settings, rating, tuning, samples that missed the level), and a trace is capped at 20 000 events and thinned on large boards, so it could not always show the final dig faithfully; the dig log is small, exact and the same for every verbosity, thread and board size. The steps built from the log showed no table for a board with no complete proof and could raise the score by jumps that nothing explained: the redesign (the user's request of 2026-10-28) gives every step a table, keeps the compact score from going down and explains the jumps that remain ([report](../docs/generation-steps/report-detailed.md)) |
| Replay analysis (§7.5) | Ratings and the explanations of jumps are computed once per result by the pure `SG.replay` (a rating is one solve with every technique under a guard of 1,000,000 checks; a count of the solutions under a guard of 200,000 nodes when the proof assumes uniqueness); the compact selection never decreases and is audited; a jump (≥ ×3 **and** ≥ +100 from a positive score) that the recorded moves cannot remove stays and is explained from partial-board probes; a board that cannot be rated gets a status (`capped`, `stuck`, `ambiguous`, `unverified`), never a guessed score or a retry; `checks` is diagnostic | The generator's recorded moves are whole symmetric groups, exchanges and signs, so a smaller step may not exist; the page never rates a board and never claims what no solver could do. `checks` differs by a few between runs because `core/js/engine/tech-subset.js` spaces its budget checks with a counter that lives across solves, and the core was left as it is |
| Cell counts (§5.1) | `sohei` 288, `flower` 189 (the first plan said 324 and 225) | Arithmetic of the geometry of §5.2, checked by the builder |
| Names | UI card = `SG.explainCard` (`js/ui/explain.js`); core data = `SG.explain` (`js/core/explain-data.js`) | They were two modules with the same natural name |
| Locale strings (§11) | `SG.localeFragments`: every file pushes `{ en, vi }` fragments; `SG.i18n.mergeFragments()` merges them; the base packs win; the strings of the static page frame are one more fragment, `js/ui/locale.shell.js` | Parallel agents never edit the hot locale files |
| i18n API (§13.11) | `setLocale` returns the code; extras `detect`, `tError`, `eventText`, `setCellLabeler`, `has`, `keys`, `formatNumber`, `formatDuration`; plural rules via `Intl.PluralRules` | Needed by the controls, the step description, the exports and the tests |
| Board view (§13.12) | Ten layers (`regions overlays word cages ineq cells values grid coords overlay`); `setLayerVisible`, `getGeometry`, pure `geometry` / `renderMarkup` / `exportSvg`; one cell = 40 board units; `setSignMarks({ hidden, removed, kept, cause })` for the Greater-Than signs of the replay (the clue or sign that causes a score jump has its own frame: `cell--cause`, a separate `rect.sign--cause`) | Export, print and the help pictures reuse the pure part without a DOM; a sign that is still a removed constraint must not look restored, so its frame is a rectangle around its gap, not a new glyph |
| JSON document (§14.1) | Additive fields: `layout.cellRow/cellCol` (non-row-major boards), `symbols`, `wordoku`, `digOrder`, `maxAttempts`, `warnings`, `stats.timings`, `locale`, `labels`, extra `difficulty` fields, and (task `T-7.8`) `model`, the plain-data model spec for the player | The document must describe the puzzle completely; rule L6 (names in the export language); the player rebuilds the board without any variant code |
| URL and storage (§14.2, §14.3) | Extra params `order attempts sol o.<id>` (the `trace` and `speed` of older versions are ignored); a URL with a `seed` generates on load; seed hashing (FNV-1a); share links carry `v d sym mode`; `sg.cache.v1` format | State that is not a puzzle parameter must not bend a shared link |
| Cache (§6.8 ⑦) | Result cache + background pool; the trace and the dig log (the replay) are **regenerated from the seed** — in a worker only: where the page has none, only after the user presses "Prepare score tables" (`replay:consent`), and `SG.app.markTraceUnavailable()` shows the fallback replay when no log can be had; *progressive difficulty (warm start)* not built | A trace can be megabytes; the guided dig already covers the warm-start need; in main mode nothing heavy may start before the user has agreed (Q19 a) |
| Multi-grid (§8.4) | Generic MRV + `fastSeedOverlaps`; no sub-grid-wise digging sequence; p95 ≈ 0.08–0.32 s at `normal` and `hard` in Node (the qualification) instead of the planned 8–20 s budgets | The oracle and the clue floors keep the digs short |
| Mega (§8.6) | Symbol set is the option `symbolSet`; **no** mobile minimap / horizontal scroll (the SVG scales to the width) | Scaling the viewBox was enough for the board sizes tested; the minimap is an idea (below) |
| Killer, Greater-Than, Wordoku (§8.2, §8.3, §8.5) | Killer's layout uniqueness check / repair runs for hard and above only (veryEasy–normal rely on the clues); GT: `build` returns the empty board, signs come from `postGenerate`, the dig removes signs (revealing clue groups where the level needs them); Wordoku letters are in alphabetical order and the VI list is short, so the EN list takes over with an event | Models are immutable; uniqueness at the easy levels is guaranteed by the oracle |
| Unique-solution check (§6.5) | `stillUnique` is ≈ 3.7–4× faster than counting on remove-stays-unique calls, ≈ 1.8× over a whole dig | The first estimate (5–12×) was optimistic; the "≥ 3×" criterion was accepted in its narrower reading |
| Order of work (§17) | P5 `T-5.1…5.8` before P4; `T-5.9`, `T-5.10` after P4 had started; the pre-generation task `T-6.3` is *not* the calibration (that is `T-2.10`) | Avoided merge conflicts on the hot files |
| Split into two repositories (`T-7.5`, `T-7.8`) | The trial-play mode (`js/ui/play.js`, `css/play.css`, `tests/suites/play.test.js`, the `sg.play.v1` key and the play shortcuts of the help) moved to the **sudoku-player** repository. This one keeps the generator and gains `js/ui/exchange.js` (shared byte for byte), the `model` block of the JSON and the "Play" button (§10.9) that opens the puzzle in the player | A player and a generator have different users and release rhythm; the only coupling left is a documented document format, carried in a URL fragment so that no server is involved |
| Tests (§15) | 56 suites, split so that development is fast: 48 quick ones (the default run, about 2,900 tests: 32 in `tests/suites/` and the 16 shared with the player in `../core/tests/suites/`) and 8 heavy ones in `tests/manual/` (`--manual` / `--all`, about 3,910 tests in all; CI runs the quick set only, §15.1) (`play.test.js` left, `exchange.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `smoke.test.js`, `replay.test.js`, `replay-recorders.test.js` and `perf-tooling.test.js` came); the harness has more matchers; the user and developer guides are checked mechanically by `help.test.js`; `tests/perf.js` + regression hashes; `tests/soak.js --full`; the replay's golden numbers are classic-script test data (`tests/fixtures/replay-goldens.js`, and `replay-wire.js` generated by `tests/replay-fixtures.js`, which `tests.html` can load from `file://`) | See §15 |
| Page layout and result panel (§10.1, §10.5; later change) | The Status box (and the mode badge) and the list of generation steps are gone. The result and the step description are two tabs (Summary / Generation steps) of one box under the board; the replay controls (with the "Show all moves" checkbox and the status block of the preparation) are in the second tab; the puzzle options (with Generate at the top) are the right column; a footer carries the copyright and the repository link. The Summary has two small cards and a "Difficulty score" table (Technique / Other sections, Total row = the score, help icons), and the step description shows the same table for the puzzle at that step, on every step (`player.tableAt`), with its badge and the callout of a score jump. The thumbnail, seed, share link, Play-online and solution controls, timings and the hardest-technique / time / attempts cards were removed from the panel | The page showed the same information three times (status, steps, result); the score is now explained where it is read, and the options no longer move when a variant has many of them |
| Saved list of puzzles and exports (§10.1, §10.6, §14.3; later change) | `SG.history` keeps every generated puzzle in `sg.history.v1` and shows it in the left panel (variant; level, score and the last 6 characters of the seed). Print and Export choose their puzzles from that list; the Print dialog has no preview and offers 1, 2 or 4 puzzles per page; Export writes readable text (`#n,variant,level,seed` + the grid rows) instead of the encoded token (`buildExportCode` / `openCode` were removed) | Several puzzles can be made, kept and printed or exported together; a readable text can be consumed by other tools, and the seed plus the clues are all a reader needs |
| Document history | This specification was translated from Vietnamese to English at the user's request; the original Vietnamese `design.md` / `task.md` no longer exist on disk | — |

### Known limitations and ideas

- **Main-thread mode** (`file://`, no Worker): slices of ≈ 12 ms, but one unsplittable piece of work can stall the page — a single DFS slice in Killer takes 70–86 ms, a Greater-Than sign dig 50–70 ms per sample, the cage layout of a hard Killer up to ~300 ms — and background pre-generation does not exist there. The replay's score tables are computed on the page there too, and only after the user presses "Prepare score tables" (nothing runs before; the replay controls stay off until the tables are ready): the analysis yields after every position, but one rating (up to the guard of 1,000,000 checks) cannot be split.
- **The replay's ratings and explanations:** a board that exceeds a guard (1,000,000 checks for a rating, 200,000 nodes for the count of the solutions) shows a status instead of a score and is not retried; the explanation of a jump describes the generator's own solution and its limited check, never what every solver needs; the work count `checks` is diagnostic and differs by a few between runs.
- **Browsers:** only Chromium-based Edge was run. Firefox 100+ and Safari 15.4+ are targets but untested; no real mobile device and **no real screen reader** were used.
- **The pre-generation switch** in Settings (`SG.cache.setEnabled`) is not persisted.
- **Weights** of rating v2 are engineering priors, not measurements of human solvers (`ruleOf45` and `cageSplit` are the first calibration targets); a strict symmetry with a very high level returns the closest puzzle with a notice.
- **Wordoku in Vietnamese** has a short word list (the English list takes over).
- **Ideas not built:** a minimap for 16×16 on phones; progressive difficulty (warm start); persisting the pre-generation preference; a "kid mode"; sub-grid-wise digging; a percentage progress bar.
